### 15k. WP1's, WP2's, WP2b's, WP2c's, WP2d's and WP2e's gates on the CP6 image

WP1's gate (plan §11): **G-FAULT (WP1 subset), G8, P-13 and the 10 s reset ×10**, here with a portal smoke on an iPhone and an Android and a leak check (items 1-6). **Pass for all of them: 0 reboots other than the 10 s reset's own, 0 panics, no invalid list or status JSON, 400s where due** (plan §12). WP2's gate (plan §11): **G3-lite (the E4 replay), the DPS router-pull test, first commissioning and P-14's LAN part** (items 7-9), each with its own pass below. WP2b's check (the user's decision of 2026-10-01: leak evaluation and the valve close never wait on the cloud; 15m) is **item 10**, read at the router pulls of items 5 and 7 plus one leak run of its own. WP2c's checks (15n; the proposal's §5.3) are **items 11-16**: boot, idle and order (11), **the LS-1 acceptance, a leak → CLOSE within 200 ms during a WAN black-hole with Wi-Fi up (12)**, the MQTT stop behind `cloud_tx`'s publish (13), normal-link regressions (14), and on bench-only images the fault image (15, **health alerts held, then delivered**) and **`cloud_tx`'s stack high-water mark** (16, T6-11). WP2d's check (15o) is **item 17**: **a leak during a held rules or provisioning mutex is acted on**, on a bench-only hold image. WP2e's checks (15p) are **item 14(g), rewritten: the last twin report written is the newest**, and in item 15(a) **SAFE-1 at 8** (health alerts held from 8 queued items, twin reports deferred, never lost). Run each first with the valve **unpowered** (provisioned, batteries out), then **linked**, where the item uses the valve hub.

**Since WP2c, read the order of two tasks' lines by the ESP log's own times.** The `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub …`, `Offline — buffering …`, `Draining …`, `OFFLINE_BUF: …`, `IOTHUB: Twin reported …` and (when `cloud_tx`'s write ends a session) `IOTHUB: Disconnected.` lines now come from `cloud_tx`, so they can print before or after `iothub_task`'s lines of the same moment (15n R11). Measure every interval below from the `(N)` milliseconds in each line, which the ESP log takes when the call is made, before it waits for the console; and take the order of messages on the wire from the IoT Hub capture. **Since WP2e every `IOTHUB: Twin reported (…)` line is `cloud_tx`'s** (15p), printed before `cloud_tx` waits for esp-mqtt's API lock: after a `rules_config` or `set_hub_name` it can print, with an earlier `(N)`, before that command's cmd_ack `Pub event` line, while on the wire the ack always goes first (the esp-mqtt task publishes it holding that lock). Neither the UART order nor the `(N)` times show that wire order. Where the IoT Hub side shows both (the cmd_ack's enqueued time in the monitor, the property's `$metadata` … `$lastUpdated` in the twin), the ack is the earlier; otherwise the order is the code's guarantee (15p), not a bench check.

**Set-up** as 15d (hub `GW-7C4FADAE69C8` on COM30 with the CP6 build, `idf.py -p COM30 flash`, never `erase-flash`; one UART file per run with `--timestamps`; the IoT Hub monitor per run; a note per phone join). Also:
- a Windows laptop with `curl.exe` (built into Windows; in PowerShell type `curl.exe`, since `curl` is an alias for `Invoke-WebRequest`), Python 3, and `dig` (in WSL: `sudo apt install bind9-dnsutils`; or a Linux or macOS machine). The laptop that remembers `WiFi-Hub-69C8` is E2's: keep it off except where a step asks for it;
- a second ESP32 that can beacon chosen SSIDs (item 4b);
- the probe script below, saved outside the repo, for example as `$env:TEMP\dns_probe.py`;
- the spare hub with no devices provisioned (item 8), on its own COM port and IoT Hub monitor (its own gateway ID);
- a PC on the home LAN, not joined to the SoftAP (item 9; the laptop can be it).

**Where to run the laptop items.** On the router-outage fallback SoftAP BLE keeps scanning, and a laptop may get no lease for minutes (15e item 3). Run P-13 and G-FAULT's DNS and HTTP items in the 10 s-reset portal (BLE paused, no credentials) or on a spare hub with no devices provisioned, and note which. The DNS answers only on 10.10.0.1, and only to senders in 10.10.0.0/24. Since WP2 (C3) the web server runs only while the SoftAP is up and answers only requests to 10.10.0.1 from a SoftAP client: item 9 checks the home LAN.

**How the SoftAP stops on this image** (15i): after a setup-page Connect, 60 s after the IP, or 15 s after the last station leaves (never before 15 s); after an automatic rejoin, 0.5 s after the IP with no station on it, or 20 s (10 s after the last station leaves) with one. The cloud is admitted only after that stop. Where an item below needs the SoftAP up after the IP, keep a phone joined to it.

**Report at once:** any `rst:` other than the 10 s reset's, `Guru Meditation`, `***ERROR*** A stack overflow in task` (the canary check is on; above all `dns_server`, `wifi_manager`, `httpd`, `iothub_task`, `wifi_task`), `ESP_ERROR_CHECK failed`, `abort() was called`, with the 30 s before it. Also esp-mqtt's `W … mqtt_client: Client asked to stop, but was not started` and `E … mqtt_client: Client has started`: either means the start/stop hand-over between `iothub_task` and `wifi_task` was missed (15m residual 3, or a start that failed, 15i residual 6). **Since WP2c and WP2d, also** (on the CP6 image; the bench-only images of items 15-17 have their own lists): a CLOSE more than 200 ms after its sensor line during a stall (item 12); an event missing at IoT Hub that is not classified as R5 or ring overflow (item 12); two `boot` or `fast` snapshots on one connect; any `TX queue full`; any `called on iothub_task - refused`; `Pub … not confirmed (msg_id=…) - not kept`; `cloud_tx: creation failed - rebooting`; `SNAP result outstanding`; `decommission: cloud_tx did not finish …` on a normal link; a stack overflow in `cloud_tx` or a heap-corruption abort; and any of WP2d's busy-lock lines (`Rules lock busy`, `Provisioning busy for 1 s`, `Boot: rules engine missed the device list or rules`), which a normal run should never print (contention nobody expected). **Since WP2e, also:** a session whose last `IOTHUB: Twin reported (…)` misses a change made in that session (a `hub_name`, `auto_close_enabled`, `trigger_mask`, `snapshot_interval_s` or device count that the UART shows applied, with the session still up about 7 s later; item 14g); `IOTHUB: Twin report N not taken by MQTT (…)` with no fresh `Twin reported` within about 7 s while still connected; `TX queue full (24) - twin not sent`, which can no longer print.

**1. Portal smoke, iPhone then Android (state S2).** After a 10 s reset (item 2's first run can be it), each phone joins `WiFi-Hub-69C8` from Settings; the sign-in window should open by itself; the page lists networks; choose the router, type its password, Connect. Expect `APP_WIFI: portal client <IP>: first DNS query`, `… captive probe (302 sent)`, `… page request`, `… Connect/Disconnect request` (each `…, N ms after joining`), `Connected! IP`, `portal priority: Wi-Fi connected - BLE scanning stays paused until the setup AP stops (about 60 s)`, `SoftAP tail after a setup-page Connect (stations on it: 1) - it stops 60 s after the IP, or 15 s after the last station leaves (not before 15 s)`, `IOTHUB: cloud admission deferred: SoftAP up - no TLS or DPS until it stops`, and the success page. When the phone leaves the SoftAP (or drops at the channel switch), `SoftAP tail: no station left on it - it stops N s after the IP`; if it joins again, `SoftAP tail: a station on it again - it stops 60.0 s after the IP`. Then `SoftAP stopped (its servers too) N s after the IP` and `portal priority OFF (AP stopped) - BLE scanning resumed` (15 s after the phone left, not before IP + 15 s, at most IP + 60 s), then `IOTHUB: cloud admitted N s after the IP (…)` and `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!`. Note the tap time, whether and when the sign-in window opened by itself, what the page showed, whether the phone stayed on the SoftAP and when it left, and the phone's model and OS (as in G0, to compare with CP5). A sign-in window that does not open by itself, or a phone that drops before it shows the success page (no CSA until WP4's C13), is recorded, not a failure here (G-CNA after WP4 has the targets); a reboot is a failure.

**2. The 10 s reset ×10.** With the STA **connected** 4 times; **idle** 3 times (on the router-outage fallback SoftAP, between two router retries); **connecting** 3 times (on that SoftAP, start the 10 s hold about 25 s after the last `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …`, so that it fires inside the next `router fallback: retrying the configured network (attempt N)` attempt; a run whose `LONG PRESS CONFIRMED` is not within 3 s after that line counts as idle, and is repeated). Each run: `RESET_BTN: === LONG PRESS CONFIRMED — CLEARING WIFI CREDENTIALS ===`, `Wi-Fi credentials erased from NVS`, `Rebooting into AP mode...`; after the reboot `portal priority ON (no Wi-Fi credentials) - BLE scanning paused`, `Wi-Fi channel at AP start: …, router not joined since boot` and `dns_server: DNS Server listening on 53/udp`, and a phone or the laptop can open the page. Set Wi-Fi up again between runs (item 1's steps). **Pass:** every run ends in the no-credential portal; no `Wi-Fi NVS lock busy for 3 s - erasing without it` and no `Wi-Fi credential erase failed`; no reboot but the reset's own.

**3. P-13 (plan §7.5).** The laptop on `WiFi-Hub-69C8` with its lease:

```bash
dig @10.10.0.1 captive.apple.com A
dig @10.10.0.1 captive.apple.com A +noedns +norecurse
dig @10.10.0.1 captive.apple.com AAAA
dig @10.10.0.1 captive.apple.com HTTPS
dig @10.10.0.1 _dns.resolver.arpa SVCB
dig @10.10.0.1 $(python3 -c "print('.'.join(['a'*63, 'b'*63, 'c'*63, 'd'*58]))") A
```

(an older `dig` that knows neither HTTPS nor SVCB takes `TYPE65` and `TYPE64`). Then, in PowerShell, `python $env:TEMP\dns_probe.py odd` (UDP datagrams of 1, 11 and 600 B). Last, once Wi-Fi is set up again from the laptop or a phone that then stays joined to `WiFi-Hub-69C8` (so the SoftAP stays up 60 s after `Connected! IP: <LAN IP>`), within that minute, from a machine on the home LAN: `dig @<LAN IP> captive.apple.com +time=2 +tries=1`.

Pass:
- A: `status: NOERROR`, `flags: qr aa rd ra`, `ANSWER: 1`, `captive.apple.com. 60 IN A 10.10.0.1`; with EDNS (dig's default, with a cookie) `ADDITIONAL: 1` and an `OPT PSEUDOSECTION` with `udp: 512`. No `WARNING: Message has … extra bytes at end` and no `malformed` (CP5's EDNS0 defect, B2).
- `+noedns +norecurse`: `flags: qr aa ra`, `ADDITIONAL: 0`, the same answer.
- AAAA, HTTPS and SVCB: `status: NOERROR`, `ANSWER: 0` (CP5 answered each with an A record).
- The 250-character name (a 291 B query with dig's cookie, under the 300 B drop): NOERROR and the A answer.
- `dns_probe.py odd`: `no reply (pass)` three times. (On Windows an ICMP "port unreachable" also reads as no reply, so run it right after the dig queries have shown the DNS up.)
- The LAN IP: no answer (`timed out` or `connection refused`). A LAN host with a static route that sends to 10.10.0.1 through the hub's STA IP gets no answer either since WP2 (`30fb932` drops senders outside 10.10.0.0/24; 15h risk 8 closed): item 9 tries it.
- **No reboot.** The hub logs nothing per query (DEBUG), only `portal client <laptop IP>: first DNS query` once.

The probe script (Python 3, standard library only):

```python
import os, random, socket, sys, time
HUB = ('10.10.0.1', 53)
Q = bytes.fromhex('123401000001000000000000') + b'\x07captive\x05apple\x03com\x00\x00\x01\x00\x01'
s = socket.socket(socket.AF_INET, socket.SOCK_DGRAM)
if sys.argv[1:] == ['odd']:            # P-13: datagrams of 1, 11 and 600 B
    s.settimeout(2)
    for n in (1, 11, 600):
        s.sendto(bytes(n), HUB)
        try:
            print(n, 'B: reply of', len(s.recv(2048)), 'B (FAIL)')
        except OSError:
            print(n, 'B: no reply (pass)')
    sys.exit()
s.setblocking(False)                   # G-FAULT: 1,000 datagrams, about 30-50 a second
per_s, t0 = {}, time.time()
def drain():
    while True:
        try:
            s.recv(2048)
        except OSError:
            return
        k = int(time.time() - t0)
        per_s[k] = per_s.get(k, 0) + 1
for i in range(1000):
    if i % 2:                          # a real query with 1-4 bytes changed, a quarter cut short
        d = bytearray(Q)
        for _ in range(random.randint(1, 4)):
            d[random.randrange(len(d))] = random.randrange(256)
        if random.random() < 0.25:
            d = d[:random.randint(1, len(d))]
    else:                              # random bytes, 1-600 B
        d = os.urandom(random.randint(1, 600))
    s.sendto(bytes(d), HUB)
    time.sleep(0.02)
    drain()
time.sleep(1)
drain()
print('replies:', sum(per_s.values()), ' most in one second:', max(per_s.values(), default=0))
```

**4. G-FAULT, WP1 subset (plan §12).** Pass: 0 panics, 0 corruption, 400s where due.
- **(a) The heap hold, by proxy** (15h "Process" item 3). The router-outage fallback with a phone polling the page while the router is power-cycled twice, then once with the E2 laptop on the SoftAP. Record every `idma:` line (`min`, `min_largest`, `min_ever`, `allocfail` and its `(last: …)`). Pass: no reboot; any `wifi_manager: AP up without its …` is followed by `AP servers running again (HTTP and DNS)` before the run ends (15g); `network list: no memory for its 1489 B …` at most once per AP start; the page lists networks again once the heap recovers.
- **(b) Beacons.** The second ESP32 beacons close to the hub (the list keeps the 15 strongest): first 10 SSIDs of 31 × 0x01 plus a distinct last byte (`A` … `J`), then 10 SSIDs of 31 × 0xFF plus a distinct last byte. Ten identical SSIDs with one auth mode are one entry since C2b, so the plan's "10 SSIDs of 32 × 0x01" alone no longer tests the bound. With the laptop on the portal, each time: `curl.exe -s http://10.10.0.1/ap.json -o $env:TEMP\ap.json`, `python -m json.tool $env:TEMP\ap.json`, and `curl.exe -s http://10.10.0.1/status.json`; also look at the page on a phone. Pass: valid JSON every time; the 0x01 networks listed as `???…?A` and so on; about 6 of the 0xFF networks listed (about 240 B each), with `ÿ` escapes and `"raw":1`, and `wifi_manager: network list: N access points left out (list buffer full)`; no reboot. Tapping a `?` network submits `?` bytes and fails to connect: expected.
- **(c) DNS fuzz.** `python $env:TEMP\dns_probe.py` (1,000 datagrams). Pass: no reboot and no stack-overflow line; the script's `most in one second` at most 20 (the reply cap); `dig @10.10.0.1 captive.apple.com A` still answered right after; `allocfail` not rising during the run. Then once more while two phones join the SoftAP (their first-DNS log lines run on the DNS task's stack).
- **(d) HTTP header fuzz** (PowerShell):

```powershell
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -H "Host: $('a'*100)" http://10.10.0.1/
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -H "Host: $('a'*2000)" http://10.10.0.1/
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -H "Host:" http://10.10.0.1/
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X POST http://10.10.0.1/connect.json
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X POST -H "X-Custom-ssid: $('s'*33)" -H "X-Custom-pwd: 12345678" http://10.10.0.1/connect.json
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X POST -H "X-Custom-ssid: test" -H "X-Custom-pwd: $('p'*65)" http://10.10.0.1/connect.json
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X POST -H "X-Custom-ssid: test" http://10.10.0.1/connect.json
```

  Expected, in order: `302` (a Host over 63 characters is read as empty and redirected), `431` (httpd's 1,536 B header limit), `200` (no Host gets the page), then `400` four times (no headers; a 33-character SSID; a 65-character password; no password, as an open network still gets until C8). Never send a valid SSID and password here: that starts a connect. Pass: these codes and no reboot.

**5. G8 (plan §12), with the WP1 additions** (valve unpowered, then linked):
1. Hub connected, router on. Router off: `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …`, `IOTHUB: cloud admission withdrawn (WiFi down)` and `IOTHUB: WiFi down — stopping MQTT client (…)`, the component's 3 retries, then `SoftAP up with saved Wi-Fi credentials (router fallback) - BLE scanning stays on`.
2. A phone joins `WiFi-Hub-69C8` and opens the page.
3. Router on: `router fallback: retrying the configured network (attempt N)`, `Connected! IP`: the AP tail starts, `SoftAP tail after an automatic rejoin (stations on it: 1) - it stops 20 s after the IP, or 10 s after the last station leaves`, and `IOTHUB: cloud admission deferred: SoftAP up - no TLS or DPS until it stops`. Record the `idma:` lines from the IP on (15g).
4. **Within 10 s of `Connected! IP` (the tail lasts 20 s with the phone on the SoftAP, 10 s after it leaves), pull the router again.** The SoftAP stays up with the STA lost. Expect `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …` and no IOTHUB withdrawal line (nothing was admitted), no `SoftAP stopped` line, then only `router fallback: retrying …` every 33-36 s (`retry deferred - the Wi-Fi setup page is open` while it polls, 5 min at most). No component retry (C5): no `Reason:` lines about 10 s apart (its `Retry Timer Tick!` is under the WARN cap, so check by timing).
5. Keep the page open 5 min and Submit ×10 (the router's correct password, at least 10 s apart; with the router off each ends "Connection failed"). At least 5 of them deliberately 0-3 s after a `router fallback: retrying …` line: expect `wifi_manager: ORDER_CONNECT_STA: esp_wifi_set_config failed (ESP_ERR_WIFI_STATE) - attempt not started` (or `esp_wifi_connect failed`), `APP_WIFI: WiFi Disconnected. Reason: 205`, and the page showing the failure at once. `Wi-Fi setup page: Connect sent - …` beside it means that no hold took effect for that Submit (15g). Do not tap Disconnect in that window (15h risk 1).
6. Router on: `Connected! IP`, the automatic tail as in step 3, `SoftAP stopped (its servers too) N s after the IP` at most 20 s later (10 s after the phone's `SoftAP: station … left`), then `IOTHUB: cloud admitted N s after the IP (…)`, `IOTHUB: WiFi up — restarting MQTT client` and the cloud back (the first IoT Hub message). Record the `idma:` lines from the IP to 30 s after `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!`.
7. Once more steps 1-3 and 6, with the E2 laptop (it remembers the SSID) on the SoftAP when the router comes back: it never leaves, so the SoftAP stops 20 s after the IP.

Pass: **0 reboots**; no automatic attempt (`router fallback: retrying …`) less than 30 s after the previous attempt's start or end, Submits included; each Submit's page result matches the log. On this image (WP2) the cloud starts only after the SoftAP's stop: `allocfail` rising in a tail, a `DPS:` line or `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` before `cloud admitted …`, or MQTT/DPS socket or connect errors in a tail are findings (they were expected on a WP1-only image, 15g). A reboot in the tail is a finding, whatever its backtrace.

**6. A leak on this image** (P11 and P14 never ran on 2.1.3; WP1 and WP2 do not touch the leak path; WP2 moved the MQTT stop onto `iothub_task`, 15i, and WP2b moves it to `wifi_task`, 15m). With the valve linked and Wi-Fi connected, wet one BLE sensor: `eleak … leak=1`, RMLEAK then CLOSE, `leak_detected` and `auto_close` in the IoT Hub capture; dry it: `rmleak_auto_cleared` about 10 s later. Note the time from `leak=1` to the valve's CLOSE: it is item 7's and item 10's baseline. Also note, by the ESP log's own times, `BLE_LEAK: eleak <MAC> — leak=1 …` to `RULES_ENGINE: AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered by ble_leak_sensor sensor <MAC>` (one `iothub_task` pass, so milliseconds): item 10's baseline for the leak task. If practical, once more with the wetting inside a G8 tail (item 5 step 6). Any difference from CP5 is a finding.

**7. G3-lite: the E4 replay (WP2; plan §12's G3 without its E2 half).** On `GW-7C4FADAE69C8` with its usual sensors, sensors dry, the valve unpowered, then linked. **Pass:** **0 failed allocations** (`allocfail` unchanged from before the router pull to 60 s after `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!`); with 0 stations on the SoftAP, **AP down ≤ 1 s after the IP and MQTT ≤ 5 s after it**; the **`lifecycle`** (`"event":"online"`) reaches IoT Hub after every rejoin; no TLS beside the SoftAP (no `DPS:` line, `WiFi up — restarting MQTT client` or `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` between `Connected! IP` and `cloud admitted …`); 0 escape lines; no reboot.
1. **0 stations, 3 times.** No phone or laptop joined to `WiFi-Hub-69C8` (forget it on each, or switch their Wi-Fi off). Router off: `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …`, `IOTHUB: cloud admission withdrawn (WiFi down)`, `IOTHUB: WiFi down — stopping MQTT client (free TLS heap for AP/captive portal)`, `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` (the same instant: the stop is asked), then `IOTHUB: MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` (about 1 s; up to 5 s if esp-mqtt saw the loss first); the 3 retries; about 30 s after the loss, `SoftAP up with saved Wi-Fi credentials (router fallback) - BLE scanning stays on`. Wait 2 min, router on. Expect, timed from `APP_WIFI: Connected! IP: …`: `SoftAP tail after an automatic rejoin (no station on it) - it stops 0.5 s after the IP`, `IOTHUB: cloud admission deferred: SoftAP up - no TLS or DPS until it stops`, the driver's `wifi:mode : sta (…)` at about 0.5-0.7 s (**AP down**; 15i "For the user's decision" item 4), `SoftAP stopped (its servers too) N s after the IP` (about 0.6-1.3 s), `IOTHUB: cloud admitted N s after the IP (internal DMA free X B, largest Y B)` (about 1.5-3 s; X about 43-50 KB, Y about 30 KB or more), `IOTHUB: WiFi up — restarting MQTT client`, `IOTHUB: Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` (**MQTT**, about 3-5 s), the drain of the events held during the outage, then the `lifecycle` and a snapshot in IoT Hub. If the driver prints no mode line there, record `SoftAP stopped` as the upper bound. Record IP → AP down → `SoftAP stopped` → `cloud admitted` → `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` separately, so that a miss can be placed.
2. **With a phone (the E4 replay proper), twice.** As G0 run A (15d): a phone joins the fallback SoftAP during the outage and uses the page; router on with the phone still joined. Expect `SoftAP tail after an automatic rejoin (stations on it: 1) - it stops 20 s after the IP, or 10 s after the last station leaves`; if the phone leaves, `SoftAP tail: no station left on it - it stops N s after the IP`; `SoftAP stopped (its servers too) …` at most 20 s after the IP, and `cloud admitted …` 0.5-1.5 s after that line (measured from it, not from the IP: item 4 of 15i's decisions), then MQTT and the `lifecycle`.
3. **ADM-3, as decided** (15i item 1, 15m: the stop runs on `wifi_task`). `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client …` and `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` now print in the same pass (the stop is asked), so the time between them is about 0 s and measures nothing. At every router pull, record N in `IOTHUB: MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` (about 1 s with the session still connected, up to 5 s if esp-mqtt saw the loss first; report the largest), and any `IOTHUB: cloud admission deferred: the last MQTT stop is still under way` with its time to `cloud admitted …`. Once, with the valve linked, wet a BLE sensor at the moment of the pull and record `eleak … leak=1` to the valve's CLOSE against item 6's baseline. Once, if practical, unplug the router's WAN with its Wi-Fi still up for about 2 min first (esp-mqtt sees the dead session only after up to about 90 s, WP2b-LS-1, and then reconnects, so a connect can be in flight: the worst case, 10-30 s; with 1 min the session may still read connected and the stop take about 1 s), switch its Wi-Fi off, and wet a BLE sensor right after `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client …`, before `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …`: record N and the leak's `leak=1` to CLOSE. **Pass:** leak to CLOSE within item 6's spread however long the stop takes (`iothub_task` no longer waits for it); N above 30 s is a finding. Radio-hold `ON`/`OFF` pairs (15h) may be missing inside a long stop: `wifi_task` prints them and is held by the stop (WP2b-HYG-2), not a radio-hold finding. If practical, also wet a sensor about 30-60 s into that WAN-down time (Wi-Fi still up, session not yet seen dead) and record leak to CLOSE: a longer time there is WP2b-LS-1's case (15m), for the user's decision. **Since WP2c (15n):** WP2b-LS-1 is decided and closed, and that WAN-down leak is item 12's case, with item 12's pass (within 200 ms). N in `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` now counts from the moment the stop got the publish gate, so it leaves out any wait behind a `cloud_tx` publish (`IOTHUB: MQTT stop waits for cloud_tx's publish`; wifi-portal council): for ADM-3's "N above 30 s" and the largest N, measure from `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client …` (or `MQTT stop waits for cloud_tx's publish`) to the `stopped on wifi_task` line, and record any `IOTHUB: Pub … took …` line in between.

Record at every rejoin: the `idma:` lines from the pull to 60 s after `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` (`min`, `min_largest`, `min_ever`, `allocfail`) against G3's floor of 8 KB / 4.5 KB (plan §8; recorded, and a miss is a finding for WP3 and WP9), and the steady `largest` once connected again against a normal boot's (CP5: 18,432 B after a normal boot, stuck at 6,400 B after a TLS start beside the SoftAP; within about 2 KB of a normal boot is the expectation). Findings: `cloud admission deferred: internal DMA free …` right after `SoftAP stopped` (the 500 ms settle may be short, 15i), an escape line (`below the heap gate`, `whatever the heap`), `cloud admission: the SoftAP's stop still not finished after 10 s …`, `ORDER_STOP_AP: esp_wifi_set_mode failed …` with no further STOP_AP within about 5 s, `Subscribe refused (…) - reconnecting` repeating for more than 30 s, any `Outbox full - … not kept`. A second STOP_AP with no second `SoftAP stopped` line is expected (a pass).

**8. First commissioning and the DPS router-pull test (WP2; plan §12's regression row "first commissioning with DPS (router pulled mid-DPS)").** On the spare hub only: never on `GW-7C4FADAE69C8`, whose provisioning the bench keeps.
- **(a) First commissioning.** `idf.py -p <spare port> erase-flash`, then flash CP6 to it (the production tool's starting state; like T4-06's alternative start, this item erases on purpose, and only the spare hub), and set Wi-Fi up from a phone as in item 1. After `Connected! IP`: the setup-page tail line, `IOTHUB: cloud admission deferred: SoftAP up - no TLS or DPS until it stops`, SNTP (`Initializing SNTP...`, `Time synced: …`) beside the SoftAP, `SoftAP stopped (its servers too) …` (15 s after the phone leaves, at most 60 s after the IP), `IOTHUB: cloud admitted …`, `DPS: No cached assignment, performing DPS registration...`, `DPS: Connecting to global.azure-devices-provisioning.net...`, `DPS: Assigned hub=… device=…`, `DPS: Cached assignment in NVS (provisioning epoch …)`, `IOTHUB: Connected to Azure IoT Hub!`; in IoT Hub (the spare's gateway ID) the `lifecycle` with `"provisioned":false` and the empty `boot` snapshot. **Pass:** no `DPS:` line before `cloud admitted`; `allocfail` unchanged; no reboot.
- **(b) The router pulled mid-DPS.** Make the next registration live again: send the spare hub test plan T4-06 step 2's `decommission` `all` (with a new `id`): it clears the DPS cache and reboots, Wi-Fi kept. So that the registration lasts long enough to interrupt, first set T4-06's DPS block on the router (TCP 8883 to `global.azure-devices-provisioning.net` dropped, or its DNS name blocked; the IoT Hub host left reachable): the registration then waits up to 60 s. After the reboot (normal boot, no SoftAP): `cloud admitted 0.N s after the IP`, `DPS: No cached assignment, performing DPS registration...`, `DPS: Connecting to …`. About 5 s later **switch the router's Wi-Fi off** (not its power, so the block can be removed while it is off). Expect `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …`, then within about 1-4 s (up to about 10 s if the DPS client was still connecting) W `DPS: DPS registration aborted after N s: Wi-Fi lost or SoftAP up - tried again once the cloud is admitted again` (N counts from `Connecting to`), then `IOTHUB: cloud admission withdrawn (WiFi down)`; **no** `IOTHUB: DPS failed (attempt n/…), retry in …` for it. Record the time from `WiFi Disconnected` to the abort line: the DPS client's stop still runs on `iothub_task`, so this is how long that task waited (WP2b-CONC-1, 15m, for the user's decision). The fallback SoftAP comes up about 30 s after the loss, with no `DPS:` line while it is up. Remove the block, switch the Wi-Fi on: the automatic tail (0.5 s), `cloud admitted …`, and `DPS: No cached assignment, performing DPS registration...` on the next pass (no back-off), then as (a). **Pass:** the abort line and no back-off line; the registration runs again at once after the admission and succeeds; no DPS TLS while the SoftAP is up; no reboot. An E `DPS registration failed (…)` with a back-off line after the pull is a finding.

**9. P-14, the home-LAN part (C3; WP2).** From the PC on the home LAN, with the hub's `<LAN IP>` from `Connected! IP: …`. **Pass:** in a tail, `403` to each request below, nothing done (the hub logs nothing for them at INFO) and the credentials intact (no `WiFi Disconnected. Reason: 8`, and after a power cycle the hub rejoins its router); after the SoftAP's stop, `000` (connection refused); no reboot.
- **In a tail.** Use a setup-page Connect (item 1's smoke, or a 10 s reset and set-up) with the phone left on `WiFi-Hub-69C8`, so that the SoftAP stays up 60 s after the IP. Within that minute, in PowerShell:

```powershell
$hub = '<LAN IP>'
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" "http://$hub/"
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X POST "http://$hub/connect.json"
curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" -X DELETE "http://$hub/connect.json"
```

  Expected `403` three times. The POST carries no headers on purpose: one that got past the check would read `400`, with nothing started. The DELETE is the forget: a `200` means the hub forgot its Wi-Fi (a finding; set it up again). In the same minute the phone on the SoftAP still loads `http://10.10.0.1/`.
- **After the stop** (`SoftAP stopped (its servers too)`): the same three commands print `000` (nothing listens on port 80 with the SoftAP down).
- **Optional, a route through the hub** (an administrator PowerShell; remove it afterwards): `route add 10.10.0.0 mask 255.255.255.0 <LAN IP>`; then in a tail `curl.exe -s -o NUL -w "%{http_code}\n" http://10.10.0.1/` → `403`, and `nslookup captive.apple.com 10.10.0.1` → `DNS request timed out` (no answer: the DNS drops senders outside 10.10.0.0/24); then `route delete 10.10.0.0`.

**10. WP2b: the leak task never waits for the MQTT stop (the user's decision of 2026-10-01; 15i item 1, 15m).** Read every time below from the ESP log itself (the `(N)` after `I`, `W` or `E`: milliseconds since boot), not from the PC's `--timestamps`. While a stop is under way no MQTT session runs, so the `RULES_ENGINE`, `OFFLINE_BUF`, `TELEMETRY_V2` and `IOTHUB` lines then come from `iothub_task`, except three: `IOTHUB: MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` and a second `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` right before it (its re-mark after a CONNECTED that raced the request) are `wifi_task`'s, and that raced `IOTHUB: Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` is esp-mqtt's task's (15m). Since WP2c the `TELEMETRY_V2` and `OFFLINE_BUF` lines of the buffering (`Offline — buffering …`, `Stored event […]`, `Pub …`) are `cloud_tx`'s, not `iothub_task`'s; `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` at the request is still `iothub_task`'s (15n).
- **(a) The stop is only asked: at every router pull of items 5 and 7 with the cloud connected** (`Connected to Azure IoT Hub!` seen since the last rejoin). From `IOTHUB: WiFi down — stopping MQTT client (free TLS heap for AP/captive portal)` to the next `iothub_task` line, `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` (the same pass): **at most about 50 ms** (UART output included). Record that gap, N, and `APP_WIFI: WiFi Disconnected. Reason: …` → `IOTHUB: cloud admission withdrawn (WiFi down)` (`iothub_task`'s wake: normally a fraction of a second; if longer, note what printed in between). Any other line due during the stop (`iothub_task`'s sensor event and rules lines, `cloud_tx`'s `TELEMETRY_V2: Offline — buffering …` and `OFFLINE_BUF: Stored event […]`) prints when it is due, before `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …` when N is long. **Fail:** a gap of about the stop's length (1 s or more) before the first `MQTT connected = false`, or that line printing only after `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …`: `iothub_task` waited for the stop. On `0aae305`, which waited, there is no `stopped on wifi_task` line at all: check the image (15j, VAL-01) first.
- **(b) A leak raised at the pull is handled at once, three times** (the valve linked, Wi-Fi and the cloud connected, all sensors dry). Switch the router off (its power or its Wi-Fi) and wet a BLE sensor as soon as `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client …` prints, so that its `BLE_LEAK: eleak <MAC> — leak=1 …` lands while the stop may still be under way. The third time, unplug the router's WAN with its Wi-Fi still up for about 2 min first (as item 7.3: esp-mqtt sees the dead session only after up to about 90 s and then reconnects, so a connect can be in flight and N reach 10-30 s), so the leak lands inside the stop; item 7.3's WAN-down leak run can be this one. Between runs dry the sensor, let the router back on, and open the valve again once the cloud is back (`rmleak_auto_cleared`, then `valve_open`). Expect from `eleak <MAC> — leak=1 …`: `RULES_ENGINE: LEAK INCIDENT latched by ble_leak_sensor sensor <MAC>` and `RULES_ENGINE: AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered by ble_leak_sensor sensor <MAC>` within item 6's leak-task baseline (milliseconds), the valve's RMLEAK and CLOSE within item 6's leak-to-CLOSE spread, `IOTHUB: Event: BLE Leak <MAC> leak=1 batt=N`, and `leak_detected` and `auto_close` kept offline (`TELEMETRY_V2: Offline — buffering …`, `OFFLINE_BUF: Stored event […]`). After the rejoin both events reach IoT Hub in the drain (`OFFLINE_BUF: Replayed […]`; a QoS 1 duplicate is acceptable). **Pass:** in every run, `leak=1` → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` and `leak=1` → CLOSE within item 6's figures, wherever `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …` falls; and in at least one run (normally the third) `leak=1` and the `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` line both print **before** `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …`, so the check has seen a leak inside a stop (if none has, repeat the third run). **Fail:** the rules lines held until just after `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …`, or a leak-to-CLOSE time that grows with N. Measure from `leak=1`, not from the wetting: a Wi-Fi connect attempt's radio hold (up to about 3 s of BLE scanning off while Wi-Fi is down, §14d) can delay hearing the sensor; that is not this check.

**11. WP2c on the CP6 image: boot, idle, the bench keys and the order (15n).** Valve linked, 4 BLE sensors, a LoRa sensor if fitted, Wi-Fi saved.
- **Boot:** `IOTHUB: cloud_tx started (stack 5120 B, priority 3)` once, before `Connected! IP` (15j). After each connect, in IoT Hub: the `lifecycle` exactly once, the twin, then exactly one `boot` or `fast` snapshot. Every `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub snapshot: {…}` is followed, within about 1 s by the ESP times but not necessarily on the next line, by `IOTHUB: SNAP heartbeat=reset interval_ms=…`. `IOTHUB: lifecycle not built in 1 s - live messages go first` should not print on a normal connect: if it does, `iothub_task`'s pass after the CONNECTED was more than 1 s late, so record what it was printing (15n R14).
- **The bench keys** (typed into `idf.py monitor`, which sends them to the hub): `d` → `APP_LORA: Stats: RX=…, ACKs=…, LastRSSI=0`; `a` → `APP_LORA: ACK DISABLED`, `a` again → `ACK ENABLED` (leave it enabled: it toggles the LoRa ACKs, 15n R12); `r` → `Restarting RX...`; on a LoRa hub `s` → `Sending Test Packet`. Each within about 0.1 s of the key, from `LoRa Task Started` on. Repeat `d` during item 12's outage, with `cloud_tx` stalled.
- **Order:** item 6's leak arrives in IoT Hub as `leak_detected`, then `auto_close`, then the snapshot that followed them (the first copy of each).
- **Snapshots under traffic** (15n R9): during a provision pulse with 4 or more sensors (item 14c), and with a LoRa sensor beaconing, `IOTHUB: SNAP trigger=…` and `SNAP heartbeat=reset …` pairs keep coming, and `SNAP result outstanding` never prints.

**12. LS-1: a leak → CLOSE within 200 ms during a WAN black-hole with Wi-Fi up (WP2c; D4; the proposal's acceptance test).** Valve linked, at least 2 BLE sensors (`<BLE1>`, `<BLE2>`), a LoRa sensor if fitted, all dry, the cloud connected for 2 min or more. **The router's firewall DROPs the hub's outbound TCP 8883** (silently: not REJECT, which would end the session at once), and its Wi-Fi stays up; if the router cannot, unplug its WAN cable with its Wi-Fi up (item 7.3's way). Note T0, the moment the DROP starts.
1. Wet `<BLE1>` at T0 + 1 s, `<BLE2>` at T0 + 5 s, a LoRa sensor (or `<BLE1>` again after drying) at T0 + 15 s, and one sensor again right after an `IOTHUB: SNAP trigger=…` line (with the twin's 60 s interval, test plan 0.9, one comes every minute). Dry each between wets as needed. Hold the outage 2 min or more: esp-mqtt sees the session dead within about 10-90 s.
2. **Expect for each wet:** `BLE_LEAK: eleak <MAC> — leak=1 …` (or the LoRa packet's line), `RULES_ENGINE: LEAK INCIDENT latched …` (the first), `RULES_ENGINE: AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered …`, the valve's RMLEAK then CLOSE. And, from `cloud_tx`, when its stalled write times out: `IOTHUB: Disconnected.` and `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` (esp-mqtt dispatches the session's end inside that write), `IOTHUB: Pub event took N.N s (msg_id=-1)`, `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub event failed (msg_id=-1)`, `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub event not confirmed (msg_id=-1) - kept for replay, a duplicate is possible`, then for the items still queued `Offline — buffering … event` and `OFFLINE_BUF: Stored event […]`, in build order.
3. **Pass during the outage:** each `leak=1` → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` **within 200 ms** by the ESP times, and the valve's CLOSE within item 6's spread; no rules line held back until a `Pub … took` line ends (its ESP time lies inside the stall); no `Rx Queue Full`; no `TX queue full`.
4. Remove the DROP. **Expect:** the reconnect, `TELEMETRY_V2: Draining …`, `OFFLINE_BUF: Replayed […]` in order, then `Pub lifecycle`, `Twin reported`, then live events (and a second `Twin reported` once the twin GET's answer is applied, 14g); exactly one `boot` or `fast` snapshot.
5. **Pass after the restore:** in IoT Hub the first copy of each event the UART shows as published or stored appears in the UART's build order, and each sensor's last event matches the UART's last. Duplicates are allowed (D3): count them by byte-identical body, keeping the first, which is the cloud's rule (TC-2, decided 2026-10-02, 15n); one whose `ts` differs by about 1 s is a pre-sync entry re-stamped at a replay, not A-1.
6. **Classify a missing event before calling it a defect** (cloud council): an event with a `Pub event:` line before `Disconnected.`, and no `not confirmed` or `Stored event` line for it, is the PUBACK-window loss R5 (15n; `d0d5284` loses it the same way), not a WP2c defect: the +1 s and +5 s wets are likely to be this, since their writes fit in the TCP send buffer. Grep `OFFLINE_BUF: Buffer full, oldest event overwritten` first (the ring holds 16). In outages under 30 s the first copies can also invert (15n R6): check each event's `Pub` msg_id.
7. Run it 3 times. **Record:** the largest `leak=1` → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` and `leak=1` → CLOSE, the largest `Pub … took`, and next to any CLOSE over 200 ms the `OFFLINE_BUF: Stored event` lines and any `Pub snapshot` line printing then (stdout and NVS, 15n R2 and R3: D4's WP3 fixes). Also repeated LoRa packets or `Rx Queue Full` (15n R19).

**13. The MQTT stop behind `cloud_tx`'s publish, and the portal (D6; 15n R17).**
- **(a)** Each of these during a WAN-dead stall (item 12's DROP, 15 s or more in, so that `cloud_tx` is likely inside a write): a router Wi-Fi power-off; a router power pull; the 10 s Wi-Fi reset. (A SoftAP start with the STA connected and the SAS renewal stop through the same gate: check them where they occur, T6-12 step 4.) Expect `IOTHUB: MQTT stop waits for cloud_tx's publish`, then `cloud_tx`'s `Pub … took …`, then `IOTHUB: MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` (no other `Pub` line between the stop's request and that line), the SoftAP tail on time and the portal reachable. While the stop waits, `wifi_task` no longer blocks, so its `router fallback: retrying …` and SoftAP-tail lines may print: record them; anything that disturbs the portal is a finding for the top-priority portal bug. **The 10 s reset** reboots about 2 s after the press is confirmed (`reset_button.c`), so the `stopped` line may never print there: its pass is no abort or heap-corruption backtrace before `Rebooting into AP mode...`, and a usable portal after the reboot. Measure N as item 7.3 says (from the request, not from the gate).
- **(b)** On item 16's image (comprehensive heap poisoning), (a)'s Wi-Fi power-off 10 times: no heap-corruption abort, no `Subscribe refused (…) - reconnecting`, and that image's `outbox at CONNECTED: 0 B` line at every reconnect (a non-zero outbox there would be R0-1's race: report it). The normal image prints no outbox size, so on CP6 itself this check is only "no abort, no `Subscribe refused`".
- A `cloud admission deferred: internal DMA free …` right after `SoftAP stopped (its servers too)` is a finding to report (the image has about 2.3 KB less at rest, 15j), not the 500 ms settle.

**14. Normal-link regressions (WP2c, 15n).**
- **(a) A quiet hub:** item 12's DROP with no other traffic; wet a sensor at T0 + 45 s: it closes at once; esp-mqtt ends the session on its own within about 30-90 s (`IOTHUB: Disconnected.`, from the esp-mqtt task).
- **(b) Decommission-all on a normal link,** on the spare hub (item 8; provision it a BLE sensor first; never on `GW-7C4FADAE69C8`): the cmd_ack, `IOTHUB: Twin reported …`, `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub snapshot: {…"reason":"decommission"…}`, `OFFLINE_BUF: Buffer cleared`, `IOTHUB: Decommissioned — restarting in 3s...`; no `decommission: cloud_tx did not finish …`. (Since WP2e the twin is skipped when 8 or more items wait, which a normal link does not reach; the connect after the restart reports it, 15p.)
- **(c) Provision 4 sensors** (`MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` T5-01): the pulse every 30 s and on each packet, the cap line, each cmd_ack before its snapshot; item 11's snapshot pairs.
- **(d) A twin `snapshot_interval_s` change** (T5-11): `IOTHUB: SNAP heartbeat=re-aimed …`, or, with a snapshot in flight, the new interval at that result's `SNAP heartbeat=reset interval_ms=…`.
- **(e)** Item 10's router pulls; the outbox-full (−2) burst where practical (`Outbox full - … kept for replay`, replayed in order); the SAS renewal (T6-12 step 4).
- **(f) DEC-14** (E-10, `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md`): a `Pub snapshot` line followed by `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub snapshot not sent - the device set or session changed during its line; built again` was not published, so it is not counted. `IOTHUB: SNAP published while the device set changed - the reconciled one follows now` is 15n R15: the snapshot right after it must show the change.
- **(g) TW-1, fixed in WP2e (15p): the last twin report written is the newest.** Read each report's values from the JSON of its `IOTHUB: Twin reported (…)` line, order those lines by their `(N)` times, and read Azure's reported twin (the portal or `az iot hub device-twin show`) after each run. Every `Twin reported` line is `cloud_tx`'s, and every CONNECTED normally gives two: the session's, after the replay and `Pub lifecycle`, then the twin GET answer's.
  - **(1) A desired change during an outage, then a reconnect with a backlog.** With the WAN down (item 12's DROP, or the WAN cable), change the desired `hub_name` (and `snapshot_interval_s`) in Azure, wet and dry a sensor, then restore. **Expect** `Draining …`, the `Replayed` lines, `Pub lifecycle`, `IOTHUB: Twin reported (…)` (the session's report, built at the CONNECTED: it can still carry the old name), and, from the esp-mqtt task, `Twin GET requested (rid=…)`, `Twin GET: applying desired properties from full document`, `Twin: hub_name = '<new>'`, then a second `IOTHUB: Twin reported (…)` with the new name. If the GET's answer was applied before the session's report was built, that one report already carries the new name and no second follows. **Pass:** the last `Twin reported` and Azure's reported twin both show the new values. (Before WP2e the GET's report went out first and the older session report overwrote it.)
  - **(2) A command right after a provision.** Re-send the 4-sensor `provision`, then `set_hub_name` (a new name) or `rules_config` (`auto_close_enabled` flipped) right after its ack, while a `Pub snapshot` line prints. **Expect** the `Twin reported` lines in build order, the last with the new value; when both commands land before `iothub_task`'s next pass, a single report covers both. **Pass:** the last `Twin reported` and Azure's reported `hub_name` or `auto_close_enabled` show the new value. A `Twin reported` printed before the command's cmd_ack `Pub event` line is expected (above), not a failure.
  - **(3) A report MQTT refuses** (where practical: a reconnect with a full replay on a throttled link, or a full hub's snapshot plus a backlog; esp-mqtt's outbox limit, 12 KB): W `IOTHUB: Twin report N not taken by MQTT (msg_id=-2) - built again after 5 s`. **Pass:** while still connected, a fresh `Twin reported` follows within about 5-7 s (a `rules_config` or `set_hub_name` made meanwhile waits for the same window), the W lines are at least 5 s apart, and Azure's reported twin ends current. **Fail:** no report follows while connected, and the reported twin stays stale.
  - **(4)** I `IOTHUB: Twin report N not sent - report M, built after it, went first` is expected when a device-set change lands on the pass that first sees a new connection: its FIFO report is overtaken by the session's own, newer one. "Went first" means report M was seen first, sent or not. A failure only if that session's last `Twin reported` has older content than the change.
  - **(5)** With 8 or more items queued (item 12's stall, item 15), a requested report waits: no `Twin reported` until the FIFO is below 8 (checked every 2 s), then one fresh report. A delay of a few seconds is expected, not a failure; `TX queue full (24) - twin not sent` must never print.
  - **(6) A leak while a twin is owed and provisioning is busy** (safety council, 15p W2): send the 4-sensor `provision` and a `rules_config` back to back, then wet a sensor at once (and, with the valve linked, its flood probe in a second run). Record `leak=1` → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` → CLOSE: within item 6's spread passes; 1 s or more is a finding for W2 (a twin build waits up to 1 s on each of its five provisioning reads).
  - **Not TW-1:** on a slow or reconnecting link Azure's reported twin can show an older value than the last `Twin reported` on the UART: esp-mqtt's QoS 1 resend delivered an older report after a newer one (15p, R2/F4, for the user's decision). Before recording a failure, check the link for reconnects or a slow round trip, and record the twin's `$metadata` `$lastUpdated` for the property.

**15. The WP2c fault image (bench only, never shipped; 15n R4, R8, R9, R-H; the proposal's item 5).** A worktree at `545b8f2` (CP6's commit; it was `fcc0979` before WP2e), built as `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` T6-11's debug image is (its own `sdkconfig` copy and size check; `PROJECT_VER "2.1.4-fault"`), with two changes:
- in `main/telemetry/telemetry_v2.c` `publish_logged()`, right after the `Pub %s: %s` line and before the `if (current != NULL …)` check: `if (gated) vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(15000));`. Every `cloud_tx` event, snapshot and lifecycle publish then holds `cloud_tx` (and the publish gate) 15 s; the replay and the twin are not delayed. Placed before the check, it exercises the STALE path (c); placed after it, it would exercise R15's window instead (expected there: `SNAP published while the device set changed - …` and an immediate rebuild).
- a heap ballast: `heap_caps_malloc(20 * 1024, MALLOC_CAP_INTERNAL | MALLOC_CAP_DMA)`, never freed, at the first `MQTT_EVENT_CONNECTED` (a static flag in `mqtt_event_handler()`), so that the boot's admission (36 KB / 12 KB) is not held by it. It puts the internal DMA heap near a full hub's at rest; if `Message not built` appears before any flood, lower it.

Runs, valve linked, at least 2 BLE sensors:
- **(a) Flood, and health alerts held then delivered.** During a held publish, raise at least (provisioned devices + 3) events, ideally 24 (wet and dry the sensors, `valve_close` / `valve_open`), and have health alerts fall in the stall: switch a BLE sensor off 10.5 min or more before it (its `device_offline`), or the valve's supply 3 min before. Expect `IOTHUB: health alerts held - TX busy, internal free … B, largest … B` once per hold, maybe `HEALTH_ENGINE: ALERT QUEUE FULL — … held for retry` at each evaluation (ratings and the fleet LED then hold their state: 15n R8). **Pass:** every leak → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` within 200 ms; the first copies in IoT Hub in UART order; **every held health alert reaches IoT Hub after the stall** (its `ts` the build time), and the ratings and the fleet LED catch up once TX is idle. **Record** idma `min_ever` and the largest block: **Fail** below G3 (8 KB / 4.5 KB) or on `Message not built`. On this image `TX queue full (24) - <event> event not sent` is expected once 24 items are queued (record the lost events: the line names them). **Since WP2e (SAFE-1 at 8, 15p)** health alerts and twin reports enter the FIFO only while fewer than 8 items wait, so `health alerts held …` prints from 8 queued items (earlier and for longer than at `fcc0979`), and `TX queue full` needs at least 16 leak, valve or rules events queued in the stall. **Also send a `set_hub_name` during the stall:** its twin report is deferred, not lost (no `TX queue full (24) - twin not sent`), and one fresh `Twin reported` with the new name follows once the FIFO is below 8. `SNAP result outstanding` is expected too.
- **(b)** Provision a sensor while a commission snapshot is in flight: the new sensor's boot or commission snapshot still fires (R1-2).
- **(c)** Decommission the valve while a snapshot's `Pub snapshot` line has printed and its 15 s sleep runs: `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub snapshot not sent - the device set or session changed during its line; built again`, and no snapshot after the ack shows the valve (R1-3; DEC-14).
- **(d)** Pull the router during a held publish with items queued, then restore: the replay, then the lifecycle, then live events (R1-5); `lifecycle not built in 1 s` should not print.
- **(e)** Decommission-all while stalled (the spare hub): `decommission: cloud_tx did not finish in 25 s - offline buffer erased here`, both erases, and after the reboot no old event replayed (R1-7).
- **(f)** `SNAP result outstanding for N s - cloud_tx busy` once, about 60 s into a flight, then the heartbeat recovers.

Report at once on this image: a CLOSE more than 200 ms after its sensor line; `TX queue full` before at least 16 leak, valve or rules events were raised in the stall (24 items queued: since WP2e at most 8 of them are health alerts or twin reports); `called on iothub_task`; any abort or stack overflow.

**16. `cloud_tx`'s stack high-water mark (`MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` T6-11's debug image, now with `cloud_tx`, `wifi_task` and `lora_task`; 15n R7).** Built at `545b8f2` as T6-11 says, plus, for item 13b, comprehensive heap poisoning in that worktree's `sdkconfig` (after its size check) and one debug line in `mqtt_event_handler()`'s CONNECTED case: `ESP_LOGI(IOTHUB_TAG, "outbox at CONNECTED: %d B", esp_mqtt_client_get_outbox_size(event->client));`. Runs: item 12 once with a full 16-entry replay into a dying session (fill the ring offline, restore, and DROP again during `Draining …`); the pre-sync store path (power-cycle the hub with the router off, wet a sensor before the clock syncs, then restore); item 14b's decommission-all; a full-hub provision (the largest snapshot, T6-10's payload); a decommission-all then a re-provision (a live DPS registration on `iothub_task`). **Gates:** `iothub_task` 2 KB or more free; **`cloud_tx` 1 KB or more free** (below that, raise it to 6,144 B before release); 1.5 KB or more on `cloud_tx` lets WP9 trim it to 4,096 B. A task list may show `cloud_tx` at priority 5, inherited (15n R2): not a finding.

**17. WP2d: a leak during a held rules or provisioning mutex is acted on (bench-only hold image; 15o).** The kept and busy paths need a lock held 1 s or more, which nothing on the bench does with a normal image. A worktree at `545b8f2`, built as T6-11's debug image is (`PROJECT_VER "2.1.4-hold"`), with two holds, both reached by a `rules_config` C2D:
- the provisioning mutex: in `provisioning_set_rules_config()` (`main/provisioning_manager/provisioning_manager.c`), `vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(4000));` right before its `xSemaphoreGive(g_prov_mutex)`;
- the rules lock: in `rules_engine_handle_config_command()` (`main/rules_engine/rules_engine.c`), `vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(4000));` right after `g_last_rules = rules;`, before its `xSemaphoreGive(g_mutex)`.

Send `{"schema":"eflostop.cmd","ver":1,"id":"hold-<n>","cmd":"rules_config","payload":{"auto_close_enabled":true,"trigger_mask":7}}` (the rules unchanged): the provisioning hold runs 4 s from `PROVISIONING: Setting rules config: …`, then the rules hold 4 s from `RULES_ENGINE: Config updated: …`; the ack comes about 8 s late on this image. Valve linked, a BLE sensor provisioned. Read the ESP times.
- **(a) Provisioning busy:** wet the sensor within about 2 s after `Setting rules config`. Expect `RULES_ENGINE: Provisioning busy for 1 s - leak from ble_leak_sensor sensor <MAC> decided on the last known devices and rules (provisioned here)`, `LEAK INCIDENT latched`, `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered`, the valve's RMLEAK then CLOSE, about 2 s after `leak=1` (before WP2d the report was dropped); the `auto_close` in IoT Hub carries the valve's upper-case `valve_id`.
- **(b) Rules lock busy:** wet the sensor within about 3 s after `Config updated`. Expect `RULES_ENGINE: Rules lock busy for 1 s - wet report from ble_leak_sensor sensor <MAC> kept, evaluated again next pass`, then at once `IOTHUB: Event: BLE Leak <MAC> leak=1 …` (its `leak_detected` is posted; `cloud_tx` sends it once the esp-mqtt task, held in the handler, lets go of its API lock); within about 100-200 ms of the hold's end `LEAK INCIDENT latched` and `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered`, RMLEAK before CLOSE at the valve; in IoT Hub `leak_detected` before `auto_close`.
- **(c)** A short dip inside one rules hold (wet, then dry about 1 s later): both kept in order; the latch and the close, then the dry; the auto-clear 10-12 s after it.
- Between runs: dry the sensor, let the auto-clear run, send `valve_open`.
- **(d) Monitor-only:** (b) with `"auto_close_enabled":false` in the `rules_config`: `leak_detected` only, no RMLEAK, no CLOSE and no `auto_close` (the retry reads the new rules). Then `true` again, without the hold's wet.
- **Pass:** every wet of (a)-(c) closes the valve (RMLEAK before CLOSE) and none is lost; no `Failed to take mutex` for a provisioned sensor; no `Rules lock busy - … is lost`; no task-watchdog line while reports are kept; the 100 ms cadence ends with the hold (no further busy lines). These times include the hold and are not judged against item 12's 200 ms.

On CP6 itself, WP2d's normal path is checked by: none of its busy lines in any run (Report at once); a monitor-only hub (`rules_config` with `auto_close_enabled:false`: a leak reports and does not close; then `true` again); `leak_reset` then an immediate wet (`rmleak_cleared` before `auto_close` in IoT Hub); a valve swap with the old valve wet (T3-06, E-04: `RULES_ENGINE: Valve replaced: old valve leak source …`, and the new valve not closed at its first link); a `rules_config` cmd_ack within about 1 s (15o residual 8).

**Send back:** each run's UART file and IoT Hub capture (the spare hub's too), the phone notes, the `dig` and probe-script outputs, the `curl.exe` codes and `nslookup` output, the `/ap.json` and `status.json` files of item 4b, the tails' `idma:` lines, item 7's timings (IP → AP down → `SoftAP stopped` → `cloud admitted` → MQTT; `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` at each pull; leak to CLOSE), and item 10's: at each pull the stop line → `MQTT connected = false` gap, `WiFi Disconnected` → `cloud admission withdrawn` and N; for each leak run `leak=1` → `AUTO-CLOSE + RMLEAK triggered` → CLOSE, and where they fell against `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …`. For items 11-17: item 12's leak → CLOSE table per run with the `Pub … took` lines and the classified misses; item 13's `waits` → `stopped` times; item 14g's final twin values (UART and Azure), any `Twin report …` lines, and its run (6)'s leak → CLOSE times; the fault image's `idma` minima, lost-event list and health-alert arrivals; T6-11's results table; item 17's `leak=1` → close times against each hold.
