### 15m. WP2b: the MQTT stop runs on `wifi_task`, never on the leak task (2026-10-01)

**The user's decision** (2026-10-01, on 15i "For the user's decision" item 1, ADM-3 / WP2-CONC-2): move the blocking `esp_mqtt_client_stop()` off `iothub_task`, which evaluates the leaks, runs the rules engine and commands the valve close, onto the hub's Wi-Fi helper task, `wifi_task` (`main/app_wifi/app_wifi.c`: it already runs the router retry and the AP tail's checks, and may be held a few seconds once per outage). Leak evaluation and the valve close must never wait on the cloud. WP2b is committed on `fix/2.1.4` after `490f3ea`: three implementer commits, then a review and two fixer commits, the docs `6528064`, and a council (3/3 SHIP, below). ADM-3 / WP2-CONC-2 is closed (15i item 1 and residual 1). **The last firmware code commit is `cbd566c`; the last that touched firmware sources is `d0d5284` (comments only), and CP6 is of it (15j).** Nothing was pushed, amended or rebased; `sdkconfig` and `build\` are untouched; each commit was compiled on its own in the scratchpad with the project's flags, with no warning.

| SHA | Item | What |
|---|---|---|
| `fd682be` | the decision | `iothub_task` keeps the admission and asks for the stop (`mqtt_stop_request()`: clears `g_mqtt_running`, counts the request in `s_mqtt_stop_req`, marks MQTT disconnected, notifies `wifi_task`). `wifi_task` runs `esp_mqtt_client_stop()` first on each pass and at that notification (`iothub_mqtt_stop_service()`; its 1 s / 5 s wait is now `ulTaskNotifyTake()`), marks MQTT disconnected again if a CONNECTED raced in, writes the request it served to `s_mqtt_stop_done` and wakes `iothub_task`. The admission waits until that stop has ended (`ADMIT_WAIT_STOP`, before the AP settle and the heap gate): no start, DPS or SAS mint races a stop in flight. A CONNECTED while a stop is pending is not marked connected and subscribes nothing |
| `e0a6c3c` | the same, for the SAS renewal | A renewal that finds the client started asks `wifi_task` for the stop, and renews (mint, `esp_mqtt_set_config()`, start) on the first pass after it; `sas_refresh()` stops nothing. The MQTT control mutex is gone: only `iothub_task` starts or reconfigures the client, only `wifi_task` stops it |
| `6efa743` | the same, for the twin report | `publish_twin_reported()` only while connected, like every other `iothub_task` publish: a device-set change while the client stops or reconnects no longer waits on esp-mqtt's API lock |
| `cbd566c` | review WP2b-LS-2 | The offline buffer's replay reads the connection before each entry and after its publish: a session that ends mid-drain no longer has the rest of the buffer erased into an outbox the stop deletes; it is kept for the next connect (a QoS 1 duplicate at worst) |
| `d0d5284` | review WP2b-HYG-3 | Comments only: `mark_mqtt_disconnected()` is called when the stop is asked, before it runs; four other comments said a loss "stops" MQTT |

**What the image does differently**
- **A link loss, or a SoftAP start with the STA connected:** `iothub_task`'s next pass prints `cloud admission withdrawn (…)`, the stop line `… — stopping MQTT client (…)` and at once `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` (the stop is asked; events go to the offline buffer from here on). `wifi_task` then stops the client and prints `IOTHUB: MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s`: about 1 s with the session still connected (its DISCONNECT, then the end of esp-mqtt's 1 s read poll), up to 5 s if esp-mqtt saw the loss first (its reconnect wait), 10-30 s with a connect in flight (esp-mqtt holds its API lock through DNS, TLS and the CONNACK, each up to the 10 s network timeout). `iothub_task` goes on evaluating leaks meanwhile. If the IP comes back first, the admission waits for that line (`cloud admission deferred: the last MQTT stop is still under way`, once per hold).
- **The SAS renewal** (about every 18 h): `SAS: within 6 h of expiry — renewing`, `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false`, `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s`, `SAS: token renewed (…)`, `Connected to Azure IoT Hub!`.
- **`wifi_task`** is held for the stop's length once per outage, SoftAP start or renewal: its router retry, AP-tail checks and radio-hold lines wait meanwhile (WP2b-HYG-2: a hold that starts and ends inside a long stop prints no `ON`/`OFF` pair). It holds no lock of the app's while it waits, and esp-mqtt's API lock only inside `esp_mqtt_client_stop()`.
- **The replay drain** stops at the entry where it sees the session ended (new W line below), and keeps that entry and the rest.

**Every esp-mqtt call in `main/`, by task (as at `d0d5284`)**
- `wifi_task`: `esp_mqtt_client_stop()` of the IoT Hub client, its only caller (`iothub_mqtt_stop_service()`): link loss, SoftAP start, SAS renewal.
- `iothub_task`: `esp_mqtt_client_init()` and `esp_mqtt_client_register_event()` (`cloud_bringup()`); `esp_mqtt_client_start()` (`cloud_bringup()`, `mqtt_resume()`, `sas_refresh()`) and `esp_mqtt_set_config()` (`sas_refresh()`), only with no stop under way, so with the client's task ended or never started; `esp_mqtt_client_publish()` only while connected: events, the snapshot and the lifecycle (`telemetry_v2.c`), the replay (`offline_buffer.c`), the twin report and the owed twin GET (`app_iothub.c`). **The DPS client** (`dps_client.c`, through `dps_maintain()` → `cloud_bringup()` → `dps_register()`): init, register, start, the wait of up to 60 s, then `esp_mqtt_client_stop()` and `esp_mqtt_client_destroy()` (WP2b-CONC-1, below).
- The esp-mqtt tasks (event handlers, inside a session): at CONNECTED the three SUBSCRIBEs, `esp_mqtt_client_get_outbox_size()` and `esp_mqtt_client_disconnect()` (a flag only); the twin GET at the SUBACK; the C2D and twin-desired handlers' twin reports and cmd_acks; the DPS client's SUBSCRIBE and publishes on its own task.
- The wifi_manager task, httpd and the rest: none.
- No path stops or destroys the IoT Hub client for good: `DECOMMISSION_ALL`, the 10 s reset and a failed `esp_mqtt_set_config()` reboot, and `mqtt_client` is never cleared once built.

**Log lines** (`functional_test.py`'s rules match none; none prints at a production-tool boot, which has no Wi-Fi; none prints a credential):
- New, `IOTHUB` (I): `cloud admission deferred: the last MQTT stop is still under way` (once per hold); `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in %lu.%lu s`.
- New, `OFFLINE_BUF` (W): `MQTT session ended - drain stopped at [%s], kept for the next connect`.
- Same text, new time: `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` at a link loss or a SoftAP start now prints as the stop is asked, right after `… — stopping MQTT client (…)`, not after the stop; at a renewal, `SAS: token renewed (…)` now prints on a later pass than `renewing`, after the new stop line. `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client (…)` and `SoftAP up — stopping MQTT client (…)` are unchanged, still from `iothub_task`.
- Removed: none.

**Memory** (the changed objects compiled at both ends with the project's flags, `-Og`; unofficial until CP6 measures it):
- `.bss` **−75 B** (`app_iothub.c`: the control mutex's 84 B buffer and its handle gone; `s_mqtt_stop_req`, `s_mqtt_stop_done`, `s_mqtt_stopper` and `s_sas_renew_stopping` added); `.data` 0.
- Flash: `.text` **+431 B** (`app_iothub.c` +320, `offline_buffer.c` +88, `app_wifi.c` +16, `telemetry_v2.c` +7), `.rodata` **+223 B**. Nothing newly linked (15j).
- **IRAM: 0.** No `IRAM_ATTR`; DIRAM `.text` stays 113,387 B.
- **Heap at rest: 0**, except once per boot about 0.1-0.15 KB at the first stop of a connected session: its DISCONNECT write is `wifi_task`'s first socket call, and lwIP gives each calling task a semaphore of its own (`LWIP_NETCONN_SEM_PER_THREAD`; `LWIP_TCPIP_CORE_LOCKING` off), kept while the task lives (WP2b-LS-3, accepted; before WP2 the wifi_manager task made the same first call). No task, timer or queue added.
- **Stack:** `wifi_task` (4,096 B) now runs the DISCONNECT's TLS write: about 1.4 KB at its deepest by CP5's frames (`wifi_task` 96 B, the stop service 48 B, then esp-mqtt, esp-tls and mbedTLS's record encryption down the AES-GCM DMA path; lwIP's send runs on the TCP/IP task), about 2 KB with an error logged inside it. `iothub_task` no longer runs it.

**The review and the fixer.** Eight findings: WP2b-LS-2 fixed (`cbd566c`), WP2b-HYG-3 fixed (`d0d5284`); WP2b-DOC-1, -HYG-1 and -HYG-2 are `6528064`'s updates of the header, 15i, 15j and 15k; WP2b-LS-3 accepted (above); WP2b-CONC-1 and WP2b-LS-1 are for the user (below).

**The council** (rtos, safety, cloud; on `fd682be` … `6528064`, against the IDF 5.5.1 esp-mqtt source): **3/3 SHIP, no blocking issue.** Checked and passed:
- The call map above, by each specialist. One addition (rtos): `mqtt_event_handler()` can also run on `wifi_task`. If the stop's DISCONNECT write fails, esp-mqtt dispatches `MQTT_EVENT_ERROR` on the caller's task, and our handler ignores that event. Its 176 B frame and esp_event's frames still count on `wifi_task`'s stack.
- The hand-over:
  - one writer per variable;
  - `s_mqtt_stop_req − s_mqtt_stop_done` is always 0 or 1, because a request needs `g_mqtt_running` and every start is gated: `ADMIT_WAIT_STOP` before `admit_now()`, `sas_maintain()`'s pending check, and `cloud_bringup()` only with no client;
  - `s_mqtt_stop_done` is written only after esp-mqtt's STOPPED_BIT. That closes esp-mqtt's own hazard: its stop sets DISCONNECTED before it waits, and its start accepts DISCONNECTED, so an ungated start could create a second client task during the old one's teardown.
- No lost wake-up: the admission polls every 1 s, and `wifi_task` runs its pass every 1 s or 5 s.
- No deadlock:
  - `wifi_task` holds no lock of the app's at the stop, which runs at the top of its pass, before `router_retry()`'s hold, lead and order;
  - radio holds are tick deadlines, so a held `wifi_task` cannot lengthen a BLE pause;
  - the DISCONNECT/ERROR dispatch race on esp-mqtt's one-slot event queue is pre-existing and cannot deadlock.
- The leak path: Phase 2's evaluation, and the valve close it queues to the valve task, never wait on the main client's stop.
- `cbd566c` fails toward a duplicate, not a loss. `6efa743` loses nothing: every CONNECTED reports the twin again.
- Hygiene:
  - the scratch compiles of the touched files have no warning;
  - no new IRAM symbol;
  - CRLF is preserved;
  - the trailers are exact;
  - `git diff d0d5284 HEAD` shows docs only.

On the two items for the user:
- **WP2b-CONC-1:** all three concur with (a). Option (c), the asynchronous registration, is required before or with any `AZURE_DPS_PROV_EPOCH` bump; otherwise every hub with devices would block leak evaluation for up to 60 s per attempt while DPS is unreachable (safety).
- **WP2b-LS-1:**
  - rtos concurs with (a);
  - safety recommends measuring it at 15k item 7.3 and weighing (b), a `network.timeout_ms` of about 5 s, in WP3 rather than WP9;
  - the cloud specialist asks for a decision before release, not only a measurement. A silent WAN outage under a connected session is a common field event, and the rules publish at the top of a pass can hold that pass's leak evaluation for about 10-20 s.

The council's bench notes. Most are already in 15k items 7.3 and 10 and in its "Report at once" list (esp-mqtt's `Client asked to stop, but was not started` and `Client has started`). Besides those:
- **`wifi_task`'s stack (4,096 B, canary check only)** now carries the DISCONNECT's TLS write at every stop. Watch every link loss, SoftAP start and renewal for `stack overflow in task wifi_task`. A high-water-mark print would confirm the margin, but it links a new IRAM function, so it belongs in a one-off bench image only (cloud).
- **Right after a stop, the heap gate may hold one 1 s pass** while the idle task frees the ended esp-mqtt task's stack, about 6 KB. That shows as `cloud admission deferred: internal DMA free …` once. It is not a finding unless it repeats (rtos).
- **`e0a6c3c` (the SAS renewal) runs only about every 18 h.** CP6 exercises it only in a soak (`MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` T6-12 step 4) or on a short-token bench image (cloud).
- **On a flapping link,** `OFFLINE_BUF: MQTT session ended - drain stopped at […]` may appear. Every leak event kept through the outage must still reach IoT Hub after the rejoin.
- **The 10 s reset:** the stop at the forget's link loss must end, with its `stopped on wifi_task` line, before the user's Connect gets an IP. The SoftAP tail still stops on time.

**For the user's decision**
1. **WP2b-CONC-1: a live DPS registration still blocks `iothub_task`.** `dps_register()` runs on it: it waits up to 60 s for the assignment (in 1 s slices, asking the abort hook), then stops and destroys its private client there, about 1-4 s in a session (its event handler sleeps 3 s between status polls holding esp-mqtt's API lock) and 10-30 s with a connect in flight at an abort. Leak items wait in their queues meanwhile. Pre-existing (before WP2 the whole registration already ran there); WP2b leaves it. **Reach on 2.1.4:** devices are provisioned only through the cloud, so a hub with devices has finished a registration and keeps its assignment cached in `nvs_prov`; `DECOMMISSION_ALL` clears the cache together with the devices, and `AZURE_DPS_PROV_EPOCH` is still 2 (since 1.8.0). A hub with devices runs a live registration only after a future epoch bump (every hub, at its first boot of that firmware) or if its DPS cache cannot be read. Options: **(a)** accept it for 2.1.4 as a recorded exception to the rule, with (c) required before or with any epoch bump; **(b)** the reviewer's narrow hand-over: at an abort, `dps_register()` returns at once and `wifi_task` stops and destroys the DPS client (removes the 10-30 s at an abort; the wait of up to 60 s stays); **(c)** an asynchronous registration: started on one pass and polled on later ones with no wait, its stop and destroy on `wifi_task`, `cloud_bringup()` only after that stop (no new task; a larger change of `dps_client.c` and `dps_maintain()`). The fixer recommends (a) now and (c) in a later WP. Measured at 15k item 8(b). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02 (WP2c's D2, 15n):** (a) for now; (c) is required before or with any `AZURE_DPS_PROV_EPOCH` bump.
2. **WP2b-LS-1: a publish on `iothub_task` can wait on the socket in a connected session.** Gating on the connection removes only the wait behind a connect in flight. If the WAN dies silently with the router up, esp-mqtt notices only after up to about 90 s (60 s keepalive); the 5,760 B TCP send buffer fills, and a publish from `iothub_task` (an event, the snapshot, or the rules event published at the top of a pass, before that pass's leak evaluation) can wait up to 10 s in its own write (`network.timeout_ms` unset: 10 s), or about 20 s behind esp-mqtt's own resend write under its API lock. Leak items arriving meanwhile, and one dequeued on a pass whose rules event blocks, wait as long. Pre-existing (2.1.3 has it). Options: **(a)** accept it for 2.1.4 and measure it (15k item 7.3's WAN-down minute); **(b)** `network.timeout_ms` of about 3-5 s (bounds each write, but also TLS's connect steps: a slow link may then fail to connect); **(c)** `esp_mqtt_client_enqueue(…, store=true)` on `iothub_task` (no socket write there; it still waits for the API lock behind esp-mqtt's own write, up to 10 s); **(d)** publishing handed to a task other than `iothub_task` (the only complete fix; a new task needs the user's approval). The fixer recommends (a) now, with (b) and (c) weighed in WP3 or WP9. **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: (d), WP2c (15n), closed:** every publish now runs on a sender task, `cloud_tx`; checked at CP6 by 15k item 12.

**Residual risks** (none blocks WP2b; the owner is in brackets):
1. WP2b-CONC-1 and WP2b-LS-1 (above). [the user; decided 2026-10-02: CONC-1 stays, with the asynchronous registration required before any epoch bump; LS-1 closed by WP2c, 15n]
2. An event replayed or published into a live session and erased at its msg_id is still lost if the session ends before its PUBACK (15i residual 7; `cbd566c` closes only the drain's window between entries). [a later release]
3. Pre-existing, theoretical: esp-mqtt sets its `run` flag from its task's first instruction, so a stop that reaches `esp_mqtt_client_stop()` before a just-started client's task has run returns at once (`Client asked to stop, but was not started`), and that client then runs with `g_mqtt_running` false. It needs a start and then a stop request on a later `iothub_task` pass before the new priority-5 task has been scheduled once. [a later WP: check the stop's result] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `e315eda`, `1762a65`, `5a3c605`; a refused stop is tried again 10 × 20 ms, and every stop settles 20 ms.*
4. WP2b-HYG-2 (radio-hold pairs inside a long stop) and WP2b-LS-3 (the one-time 0.1-0.15 KB): documentation only (above, 15j, 15k item 7.3).
5. **A narrow window at CONNECTED** (new with WP2b; council, rtos and cloud). The handler reads no stop pending, marks the session connected, sends its three SUBSCRIBEs, and only then checks again for a stop. If a stop is asked inside that window, one `iothub_task` publish can still read "connected". It then either waits for the API lock behind `wifi_task`'s DISCONNECT write (short on a fresh session) or goes into the outbox that the stop deletes. This needs a CONNACK within milliseconds of a link loss, a SoftAP start or a renewal. [a later WP: check for a pending stop again right after the marks, before the SUBSCRIBEs]
6. **Residual 3 can also happen within one pass** (council, safety). `admit_now()` → `mqtt_resume()` starts the client while the token is just outside the renewal margin. If the clock crosses that margin before `sas_maintain()` runs in the same pass:
   - the stop is asked microseconds after the start, and can return "not started";
   - `sas_refresh()` then calls `esp_mqtt_set_config()` on a running client: `iothub_task` waits for the API lock behind a connect in flight, and the buffers are reallocated under the live task.

   After a link loss, residual 3's orphaned client also keeps trying TLS beside the SoftAP, until the next admission's start fails with `Client has started` (rtos). Negligible: it needs a 1 s boundary within about 1 ms, and a new priority-5 task that has not yet run. [a later WP: `iothub_mqtt_stop_service()` checks `esp_mqtt_client_stop()`'s result and retries a few times about 10 ms apart before writing `s_mqtt_stop_done`; fold in 15i residual 6]
7. **`wifi_task`'s `xTaskCreate()` result is not checked** (`app_wifi.c`, about line 1017). If that call ever failed at boot, the first stop would never be served, and the admission would wait in `ADMIT_WAIT_STOP` until a reboot. Leak protection would be unaffected; the cloud would stay down. Before WP2b, only the router retry and the AP tail would have been lost. This is practically impossible at boot. [WP10 register]
8. **A drain cut short leaves `s_replay_owed` false** (council, safety). On the pass where the next CONNECTED lands, a live event published after the lifecycle block can overtake events still in the buffer. The order changes; nothing is lost, and every event carries its own `ts`. [with 15i residual 4]
9. **The renewal now stops before it mints** (council, cloud). A failed mint (no memory) leaves the client stopped until a later pass mints; before, it left the old session up. A stopped session frees about 20 KB, so this is unlikely. Not a defect. [none]
10. **Cosmetic** (council, cloud and rtos):
    - `WiFi down — stopping MQTT client` does not print for a loss during a renewal's stop, because `g_mqtt_running` is already false;
    - `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …` prints even when the stop returned `ESP_FAIL` (esp-mqtt's own W line comes first);
    - `MQTT connected = false` prints at every request, even when MQTT is already disconnected.

    [WP10 register]

**Documents still to follow** (WP10 register, with 15i's): `RADIO_PORTAL_PLAN.md` §4.6 says "`iothub_task` stops MQTT at once on a link loss" (now it asks, and `wifi_task` stops); `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md`'s link-loss steps (T4-10 A2, the G8 rows) do not show `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task …` yet; its SAS soak step (T6-12 step 4) does since `6528064`; 15k item 10 (WP2b's check) is not in it yet.

### 15n. WP2c: every cloud publish leaves the leak task for a sender task, `cloud_tx` (2026-10-02)

**Why.** WP2b-LS-1 (15m "For the user's decision" item 2): a publish on `iothub_task` could wait about 10-20 s when the WAN died silently under a connected session (esp-mqtt's own write into a full TCP send buffer, or esp-mqtt's API lock behind its own write), and every leak item waited with it. CP5 measured 10-20 s from a leak to its CLOSE during a WAN black-hole. The user asked for the complete fix before CP6: no network term between a leak and its CLOSE.

**The user's decisions** (2026-10-02, on the WP2c proposal; the proposal, its synthesis and its evidence map are in the session scratchpad, `C:\Users\antun\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\c--Work-Projects-EfloStop-2-Firmware-Production-eFloStop-WiFiHub-idf1\ee520eed-0dc0-45ef-bdfe-91bf0b44762d\scratchpad\council\wp2c\` (`WP2C_PROPOSAL.md`, `synthesis.md`, `evidence.md`); the scratchpad does not outlive the session, so the design is restated here):
- **D1 (i): a static sender task, `cloud_tx`** (5,120 B stack, priority 3, no core affinity), and `uart_cmd_task` removed, its bench keys `s`, `r`, `d` and `a` polled without waiting from `lora_task`. The task count is unchanged and no heap is held at rest, but `.bss` grows by about 6.3 KB: **an approved exception to plan invariant I10 and to plan §10's `.bss` gate** (`RADIO_PORTAL_PLAN.md`, notes of 2026-10-02).
- **D2: a live DPS registration stays on `iothub_task`** (WP2b-CONC-1, 15m), deferred to a later WP that is **required before or with any `AZURE_DPS_PROV_EPOCH` bump**. On 2.1.4 a hub with devices reaches a live registration only after such a bump or with an unreadable `nvs_prov` cache.
- **D3: at-least-once delivery (A-1).** An event whose publish sees its session end is also kept for the replay, so a QoS 1 duplicate is possible, and it can arrive after newer events. **The cloud must dedupe on `gateway.id + ts + event + device id`, keeping the first copy** (D3's first text; **superseded on 2026-10-02 by the user's decision on the review's TC-2, below: the cloud drops a message only when it is byte-identical to one it already received (the payload, or a hash of it), keeping the first copy**; written for the cloud team in `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5, with the pre-sync exception).
- **D4: the local terms left on the leak path** (stdout, NVS and flash) are measured at CP6 (15k item 12). WP3 fixes them only if a leak → CLOSE exceeds 200 ms: RMLEAK and CLOSE posted before the two rules log lines (text kept, order changed), and/or the incident latch persisted after the posts (the RAM latch still first).
- **D5: WP2d right after WP2c** (`rules_engine_evaluate_leak` dropped a wet report on a 1 s lock timeout): 15o.
- **D6 (i): the MQTT stop stays on `wifi_task`,** behind a try-only publish gate.
- **D7: CP6 is built after WP2c, plus WP2d if it cleared review:** it did, so CP6 was of `fcc0979` (15j); since WP2e it is of `545b8f2` (15p).

So WP2b-LS-1 is decided and closed, by 15m's option (d) (publishing handed to a task other than `iothub_task`).

Each was compiled on its own in the scratchpad with the project's flags (`-Og -Wall -Werror=all -Wextra`, objects outside `build\`), with no new warning. Nothing was pushed, amended or rebased; `sdkconfig`, partitions, NVS keys and `build\` are untouched; CRLF kept.

Events, the replay, every offline-buffer store, the pre-sync stamp, the lifecycle, the twin and the twin GET retry move to `cloud_tx`. The session generation replaces `g_needs_lifecycle`. Decommission-all through the FIFO (twin, FINAL snapshot, clear), the 25 s park and the erase fallback (`offline_buffer_erase_for_restart()`). `telemetry_v2_publish_snapshot()` becomes `telemetry_v2_post_snapshot()`. **From here `iothub_task` makes no esp-mqtt publish** |

A change seen after the write gives OK_LATE: the normal OK bookkeeping, and the reconciled snapshot is made due at once. `devset_changed_c2d()` raises the device-set flag before it counts the sequence |
