| `d5be8da` | review CONC-3 | While it holds live items for the lifecycle, `cloud_tx` sleeps until that hold's own deadline |
| `4ed8c77` | review TC-3, BM-3, SAFE-1 | An event whose drain ended the session prints `Offline — buffering … event` again, as at `d0d5284`; `TX queue full` names the lost event; the FIFO-full comment says up to 16 of the 24 can be health alerts |

**What the image does differently**
- **`iothub_task`** keeps every safety function (intake, membership, the rules engine, RMLEAK then CLOSE, the valve link, health, device-set changes, the snapshot scheduler) and every build: each event, snapshot, lifecycle and twin is built there, its `ts` stamped at the build. It hands each one over with a 0-timeout queue send, a single-writer flag or a try-take, and never waits on `cloud_tx`. It makes no esp-mqtt publish, never takes the publish gate, and calls no offline-buffer function after boot except the decommission fallback's erase. **On a hub with devices and a DPS cache, the path from a leak item to the CLOSE post has no esp-mqtt call and no wait on a lock that anyone holds across a network write.** What is left is local and pre-existing: stdout, NVS and flash, the rules, provisioning and health mutexes (D4; their 1 s timeouts are WP2d's, 15o), and a live DPS registration on uncached hubs (D2).
- **`cloud_tx`** makes every publish of `iothub_task`'s messages and every store into the offline buffer. Each session: the replay first, then up to 1 s holding live items for that session's lifecycle, then the lifecycle, the twin, and live items in build order. While connected: the replay every 10 s while owed or while anything is buffered, the twin GET retry every 5 s, the pre-sync stamp when owed. A session that begins mid-round sends the loop back to its session work before the next item; an event already taken for the old session fails the gate's generation check and is stored, then sent by the new session's replay. A publish that held `cloud_tx` 1 s or more prints W `Pub %s took …`: the LS-1 evidence.
- **The publish gate.** Every `cloud_tx` publish (events, the replay, the snapshot, the lifecycle, both twin kinds, the twin GET retry) runs under `s_pub_gate`, with "connected and no stop asked" checked under it; so does `wifi_task`'s `esp_mqtt_client_stop()`, including its wait for esp-mqtt's task to end. Both sides only try-take it. A stop that finds it taken logs `MQTT stop waits for cloud_tx's publish` and returns; `cloud_tx` wakes `wifi_task` when it gives the gate back, so the stop runs at most one publish later. This closes the proposal's R0-1: esp-mqtt's task frees its outbox without its API lock as it stops (IDF 5.5.1 `mqtt_client.c`), which a publish from another task could otherwise meet. The esp-mqtt task's own publishes (cmd_ack, C2D twin replies, the twin GET at SUBACK) need no gate: the stop ends that task. (Since WP2e only the cmd_ack and the twin GET: every twin report goes through `cloud_tx`, 15p.)
- **Snapshots** are built only into an idle TX (both queues empty and `cloud_tx` not working, held by a try-take of the busy mutex while it builds), one in flight. `cloud_tx` publishes it and returns OK, FAILED (not up, or refused) or STALE (its session or device set changed: no floor, rebuilt from the reconciled table). The bookkeeping runs when `iothub_task` reads the result: `SNAP heartbeat=reset …` (OK; the heartbeat is still re-armed only on msg_id ≥ 0) or `SNAP heartbeat=suppressed (publish-failed)` (FAILED, the 5 s floor) now print then, after the `Pub snapshot` line and not necessarily next to it. EVENT requests made during a flight are kept in `s_snap_next` and requested again after the result; BOOT, FAST and COMMISSION are re-armed from state every pass. While a due snapshot is held, `iothub_task` polls every 2 s, and `cloud_tx` wakes it when it goes idle. A result still missing after 60 s prints W `SNAP result outstanding …` once.
- **Health alerts** (`device_offline`, `device_recovered`) are taken from the health engine only while fewer than 16 items wait in the FIFO (**8 since WP2e**, SAFE-1 decided, a limit twin reports share: 15p) and either TX is idle or internal DMA-capable heap has 12 KB free with a 4.5 KB block. Otherwise one alert waits on `iothub_task` and the rest in the health engine's queue, losslessly (`health alerts held …` once per hold); each is built when TX goes idle or the heap recovers.
- **A full FIFO** (24 items queued, so only inside a stall): E `TX queue full (24) - <event> event not sent`, the event lost; the next snapshot carries the state, and RMLEAK and CLOSE are not affected. Up to 16 of the 24 could be health alerts (SAFE-1, below; since WP2e at most 8 are health alerts or twin reports, so at least 16 slots stay for leak, valve and rules events). A twin lost there was reported again at the next CONNECTED (since WP2e a twin waits for room instead, 15p); the decommission's clear marker is retried every 100 ms.
- **Decommission-all:** the cmd_ack (esp-mqtt task), then on `iothub_task` the valve wait and the apply, and the twin, the FINAL snapshot and the clear go through the FIFO in that order (L17). `cloud_tx` publishes them, clears the buffer (`Buffer cleared`) and reports done; `iothub_task` waits up to 25 s. If `cloud_tx` has not finished, it freezes TX (nothing more is published, stored, drained or stamped), erases the offline buffer's NVS namespace without its lock, logs W `decommission: cloud_tx did not finish in 25 s - offline buffer erased here`, and erases again just before the restart. Then `Decommissioned — restarting in 3s...` as before.
- **The bench keys** `s` (a LoRa test packet), `r` (restart RX), `d` (counters), `a` (toggle the LoRa ACKs) run on `lora_task` from `LoRa Task Started` on, and keep working while `cloud_tx` is stalled; `s` blocks `lora_task`'s RX for the packet's airtime, as it did through `lora_mutex`. The production tool sends none of them (`functional_test.py` writes nothing to the serial port).

**Tasks and locks (at `fcc0979`)**

| Task (stack / priority) | `d0d5284` | Now |
|---|---|---|
| `iothub_task` (10,240 B heap / 5) | Every safety function, plus every publish, the replay, the lifecycle, the twin, the twin GET, the stores and DPS. Waited on esp-mqtt's API lock (no timeout), the offline buffer's lock (1 s), provisioning, rules, health, sensor_meta, NVS and stdout | Every safety function and every build. Hands over with 0-timeout posts and a try-take of the busy mutex; never the publish gate, esp-mqtt publish or (after boot) the offline buffer. Waits on provisioning, rules, health, sensor_meta, NVS, stdout and `net_status`; on a live DPS registration for uncached hubs only (D2) |
| **`cloud_tx`** (5,120 B static / 3) | – | Lock order: the busy mutex (blocking, only behind an `iothub_task` snapshot build) → the offline buffer's lock → the publish gate (try) → esp-mqtt's API lock. Also NVS, stdout, and `net_status`'s mutex through the DISCONNECTED handler that esp-mqtt dispatches on `cloud_tx` when its own write fails. No provisioning, rules, health or sensor_meta lock |
| `wifi_task` (4,096 B / 5) | The MQTT stop (WP2b) | The publish gate (try) → the API lock |
| esp-mqtt task (6,144 B / 5) | C2D, cmd_ack, twin replies | Unchanged: API lock → provisioning, rules, meta; API lock → offline buffer (try-store only). Since WP2e it builds and publishes no twin report: it counts a request and wakes `iothub_task` (15p) |
| `lora_task` (10,240 B / 4) | Intake | Intake and the key poll |
| `uart_cmd_task` (4,096 B heap / 5) | Bench keys | **Removed** |

No cycle: every edge from `iothub_task` or `wifi_task` into the busy mutex or the publish gate is a try-take, and the esp-mqtt task's edge into the offline buffer is a try-store. Every gate taken is given back by the task that took it.

**Contracts** (proposal §3.2; each checked by the council at `4ed8c77`, and unchanged by WP2d):
- RMLEAK before CLOSE: untouched (one `ble_cmd_queue`, posted in Phase 2).
- Cause before consequence: one builder in build order, one FIFO, one consumer, drain-first before every event; `iothub_task` never diverts an event.
- F-08: the tick's rules event is built and posted at the take, before Phase 2, first among the pass's events, also on the reconnect pass.
- Replay, then lifecycle, then live: the generation read once per round, session work before every item, the lifecycle hold of at most 1 s.
- The settle barrier, E-10 and the single build site: at the build on `iothub_task`, plus the session and device-set check right before the write.
- Snapshot ownership; the heartbeat re-armed only on msg_id ≥ 0 (OK and OK_LATE only); one boot or fast snapshot per connect (the epoch bumps at the session reset, a provision and an emptied hub).
- The commission pulse: unchanged; requests during a flight are merged.
- cmd_ack before its snapshot: still synchronous on the esp-mqtt task. Since WP2e also before its twin report (15p).
- Event `ts` at the build; pre-sync carried as a flag.
- Lifecycle once per CONNECTED: a second copy for a session is dropped; one MQTT refused is rebuilt every 5 s.
- The twin at every device-set change and every CONNECTED (since WP2e also: the last report written is the newest built, 15p).
- `telemetry_v2_is_connected()`: same answer (now an acquire load).
- NVS and OTA: no new key, format or partition; A-1 stores entries in the existing format, so a rollback to 2.1.3 reads the same ring.

**Log lines** (every format string diffed against `7348a9a`: every existing line is byte-identical, none removed or reworded; `functional_test.py`'s version, `App version`, Gateway ID, Wi-Fi MAC, LoRa and NimBLE patterns match none of the new lines; none logs a credential):
- New, `IOTHUB`: I `cloud_tx started (stack %u B, priority %u)` (at boot); E `cloud_tx: creation failed - rebooting` (unreachable: static buffers); W `Pub %s took %lu.%lu s (msg_id=%d)` (a `cloud_tx` publish of 1 s or more; printed after the gate is given back, and its time excludes the `Pub` line itself); I `MQTT stop waits for cloud_tx's publish` (once per stop); W `lifecycle not built in 1 s - live messages go first`; W `health alerts held - TX busy, internal free %u B, largest %u B` (once per hold); W `SNAP result outstanding for %lu s - cloud_tx busy` (once per flight, after 60 s); W `SNAP published while the device set changed - the reconciled one follows now`; W `decommission: cloud_tx did not finish in %d s - offline buffer erased here`; E `%s called on iothub_task - refused` (`send_twin_reported`, `twin_get_send`). (WP2e adds I `Twin report %u not sent - report %u, built after it, went first` and W `Twin report %u not taken by MQTT (msg_id=%d) - built again after %d s`, 15p.)
- New, `TELEMETRY_V2`: W `Pub %s not confirmed (msg_id=%d) - kept for replay, a duplicate is possible`; W `Pub %s not confirmed (msg_id=%d) - not kept`; E `TX queue full (%d) - %s not sent`, printed as for example `TX queue full (24) - leak_detected event not sent`; W `Pub %s not sent - the device set or session changed during its line; built again`; E `%s called on iothub_task - refused` (`send_str`, `telemetry_v2_drain_offline`). The `called on iothub_task` lines must never print.
- **Same text, now printed by `cloud_tx`:** `TELEMETRY_V2: Pub %s: %s` and `Pub %s failed (msg_id=%d)`, the `Outbox full - …` lines, `Offline — buffering %s event`, `Draining %d offline event(s) before lifecycle...` and `Offline drain complete: …`, `IOTHUB: Lifecycle not taken by MQTT - …`, `IOTHUB: Twin reported (%d): %s`, `IOTHUB: Twin GET requested (rid=%d)` (on a retry), every `OFFLINE_BUF` line (`Stored event`, `Draining %d buffered event(s)...`, `Replayed`, `Buffer cleared`, `MQTT session ended - drain stopped …`), and `IOTHUB: Disconnected.` with `TELEMETRY_V2: MQTT connected = false` when `cloud_tx`'s own write ends the session. **Their place among `iothub_task`'s lines changes** (R11): read the order of the two tasks' lines from the ESP log's own `(N)` times or from the IoT Hub capture, not from adjacency on the UART.
- **Same text, new time:** `IOTHUB: SNAP heartbeat=reset interval_ms=%lld` and `IOTHUB: SNAP heartbeat=suppressed (publish-failed)` print when `iothub_task` reads the snapshot's result; `IOTHUB: Decommissioned — restarting in 3s...` after `cloud_tx` has cleared the buffer, or after the 25 s fallback. `APP_LORA`'s `Sending Test Packet`, `Restarting RX...`, `Stats: RX=%ld, ACKs=%ld, LastRSSI=%d` and `ACK %s` print from `lora_task`.

**Memory** (object files at `-Og` with the project's flags, `7348a9a` against `4ed8c77`; CP6 measures the link):

| Object | `.text` | `.rodata` | `.bss` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `telemetry_v2.c` | +2,186 B | +396 B | +585 B |
| `app_iothub.c` | +4,405 B | +725 B | +5,716 B |
| `offline_buffer.c` | +42 B | 0 | 0 |
| `app_lora.cpp` | −27 B | −16 B | 0 |
| **Total** | **+6,606 B** | **+1,105 B** | **+6,301 B** |

- `.data` 0. **IRAM 0:** no `IRAM_ATTR`, no `.iram` section, no `__atomic_*` library call (plain loads and stores with `memw`). The IRAM functions it calls were all linked in CP5 already (`xTaskCreateStaticPinnedToCore` 0x40387ef0, `xQueueGenericCreateStatic` 0x40384078, `xQueueCreateMutexStatic` 0x403843e4, `xQueueSemaphoreTake`, `xQueueReceive`, `uxQueueMessagesWaiting`, `xTaskGenericNotify`, `ulTaskGenericNotifyTake`), and so were the flash ones (`heap_caps_get_free_size`, `heap_caps_get_largest_free_block`, `nvs_erase_all`, `uart_read_bytes`, `cJSON_GetStringValue` at 0x42051258). DIRAM `.text` should stay exactly 113,387 B. `offline_buffer.c` no longer references `esp_mqtt_client_publish`; `telemetry_v2_publish_snapshot` and `telemetry_v2_publish_lifecycle` are gone (renamed).
- **`.bss` +6,301 B:** `cloud_tx`'s stack (5,120 B) and TCB, the FIFO's storage (24 × 12 B) and the session queue's with their two `StaticQueue_t`, the busy mutex and the publish gate, the flight context, `s_snap_next` and the flags. Plan §10's gate (+450 B) is exceeded on purpose: the I10 exception approved under D1.
- **Heap at rest:** `uart_cmd_task`'s stack, TCB and allocation headers, about 4.4-4.5 KB, are freed, so internal RAM in use at rest is about **+1.8 KB** net (WP2d adds about 0.48 KB of statics, 15o; WP2e 29 B, 15p). The proposal's estimate was +1.9 KB. If T6-11 measures 1.5 KB or more free on `cloud_tx`, WP9 may trim its stack to 4,096 B (about +0.8 KB net).
- **Peak heap during a stall:** up to 24 queued events of at most 512 B each (a realistic flood is 10 or fewer, about 4.5 KB); a snapshot is built only into an idle TX, so the large-message profile is `d0d5284`'s. Measured at CP6 (15k item 15a).
- **Flash:** about +6.6 KB `.text` and +1.1 KB `.rodata` in the objects, above the proposal's 4-5 KB estimate (the snapshot result handling, the guards and the review fixes); CP6's link with `--gc-sections` decides.
- **Stacks** (frames from the `.su` files; no high-water mark until T6-11): `cloud_tx`'s own frame is 64 B; its deepest estimated path, a drain into a dying session (task 64, service 48, send_event 64, `send_str` 80, drain 32 + 32, `drain_locked` 608, replay 32, then the publish, the abort and the DISCONNECTED dispatch about 400, the handler 176, `ESP_LOGx` about 1,330, and the interrupt frame), is about 3.2 KB of 5,120 B, leaving about 1.9 KB. `iothub_task`'s frame grows 608 → 624 B; its deepest path is still the snapshot build, about 4.2 KB, and the publish chain has left it. `lora_task` gains 64 B for the poll plus a log chain of about 1.3 KB on its 10,240 B. `wifi_task`'s stop service frame is unchanged (48 B).

**The review and the fixer.** The review's 13 findings:
- **SAFE-1** (nit): health alerts are admitted while fewer than 16 items wait, so 16 alerts plus 8 other items fill the FIFO, and as few as 8 leak, valve or rules events can then be lost in one stall. The comment is corrected (`4ed8c77`); the knob (`TX_HEALTH_MAX_QUEUED`, `telemetry_v2.c`) is the user's (below). **Decided on 2026-10-02: 8, done in WP2e** (`c71249e`; `63c2252` makes twin reports share it, as `TELEM_TX_DEFERRABLE_MAX` in `telemetry_v2.h`; 15p).
- **CONC-1** (minor): a device-set change acked while a full-hub snapshot's `Pub` line printed (about 0.9 s) could be followed by that stale snapshot. Fixed by the second check and OK_LATE (`dd19ec0`); one window is left (residual R15).
- **CONC-2, CONC-3, TC-1, TC-3, BM-3** (nits and a minor): fixed (`dd19ec0`, `d5be8da`, `ddfd25a`, `4ed8c77`).
- **TC-2** (minor): the D3 dedupe key merges distinct messages: the user's (below). **Decided on 2026-10-02: the identical payload.**
- **TC-4** (nit): a first lifecycle that fails lets live events go out before it, after a 5 s retry, as at `d0d5284`. Accepted as residual R14 (fixer, safety, cloud): the realistic causes (low heap right after the connect, a session queue full behind a stuck `cloud_tx`) do not clear within 1 s, and holding live events longer is the only real fix.
- **BM-1, BM-2** (test plan and docs): this commit (15k item 15a's flood; the new-line list above).

**The council** (rtos, safety, cloud, wifi-portal; on `7348a9a` … `4ed8c77`, against the IDF 5.5.1 esp-mqtt sources): **4/4 SHIP, no blocking issue.** Checked and passed:
- Every successful `iothub_pub_begin()` is paired with exactly one `iothub_pub_end()` on the same task, on every path (the stale and NOT_CURRENT snapshot branches, the guard paths), so the mutex is always given by its holder and no `cloud_tx` publish runs outside the gate. `wifi_task` holds the gate for the whole of `esp_mqtt_client_stop()`, including the wait for STOPPED_BIT, which covers esp-mqtt's unlocked outbox delete (rtos, wifi-portal).
- No lock cycle, no spin and no lost wake: `iothub_task` only try-takes the busy mutex and never the gate; `cloud_tx_wait()` drops to one tick only once the lifecycle hold has expired; the retry deadlines move forward when used; a kick lost before the consumer is set is covered by the 5 s poll, and `wifi_task` polls every 1-5 s (rtos).
- CONC-2's ordering holds: `compile_commands.json` has no `-mno-serialize-volatile`, so GCC's default puts a `memw` before each volatile access (rtos).
- The leak path never waits on the network: Phase 2 still runs before `net_maintain`, `dps_maintain` and `sas_maintain`; every hand-over is a 0-timeout send; `rules_engine.c`, `app_ble_valve.c`, `health_engine.c`, `app_wifi.c`, `sdkconfig` and the partitions have no change; every way a leak, valve or rules event can be lost prints a line (safety).
- Every §3.2 contract holds; every duplicate WP2c can produce is byte-identical (the A-1 copy is the published text, a non-pre-sync replay publishes it unchanged, esp-mqtt resends its own copy); the four fixer commits do what they say (cloud).
- `app_wifi.c` is unchanged and `wifi_task` waits less than at `d0d5284` (it no longer blocks on the API lock behind a `cloud_tx` write); its router retry and AP tail are not cut short; the bench keys are equivalent and now safer (`sendAck` is touched by one task); a scratch compile at HEAD has only `app_lora.cpp`'s three old warnings (wifi-portal).

**For the user's decision**
1. **TC-2: the dedupe key** (D3's text). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: (a), the identical payload** (the cloud drops a message only when it is byte-identical to one already received; `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 rewritten, with the pre-sync exception and the option to treat copies identical but for a `ts` at most 1 s apart as one; the A-1 comment, `34f3825`, now at `telemetry_v2.c:346-352`). The text below is the question as it was put. `gateway.id + ts + event + device id` merges distinct messages: two `cmd_ack`s in the same second (they carry no device id; they differ only in `data.id` and `data.cmd`), two `valve_state_changed` in the same second (they differ in `data.valve_state`), and two `auto_close` for one sensor in one second (a WP2d retry and a `cause:"reconnect"` one, 15o). The cloud could then drop a valve transition, or the app retry a command it already got. Every duplicate the firmware produces is byte-identical (one pre-existing exception: a pre-sync entry whose NVS stamp failed is re-stamped from RAM at each replay, so its `ts` can differ by about 1 s). **(a)** dedupe on the identical payload, or a hash of it (recommended by the fixer and by the rtos, safety and cloud specialists); **(b)** keep the key and add `data.id`, `data.cmd`, `data.valve_state` and `data.cause`. Optional later: a per-message sequence number in the envelope, assigned at the build (A-1 copies would inherit it). Until decided, `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 gives the cloud team D3's rule with this caution; the A-1 comment at `telemetry_v2.c:344-345` follows the choice.
2. **SAFE-1: `TX_HEALTH_MAX_QUEUED`** (16). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: 8, now** (WP2e: `c71249e`; since `63c2252` the limit is `TELEM_TX_DEFERRABLE_MAX` in `telemetry_v2.h`, and twin reports share it; 15p). At 16, as few as 8 leak, valve or rules events fill the FIFO in one stall when 16 health alerts are queued (R4). The safety specialist recommends **8**, which keeps at least 16 slots for safety events; losing one of those loses no safety decision (CLOSE is decided and posted locally; the next snapshot carries the state). Now, or in WP9 after CP6's flood (15k item 15a).
3. **TW-1 (cloud specialist, found at the council, new in WP2c): a stale twin report can be the last one written.** **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: fixed before CP6, done in WP2e** (15p: one build site on `iothub_task`, one ordered sender, a build number checked at the send; 221 lines added and 64 removed in three files against `6d8f7ec`, much of it comments, rather than about 15). Bench check: 15k item 14(g), rewritten. The text below is the finding as it was put. `iothub_task` builds the device-set twin when it posts it and the session twin at its reset, but `cloud_tx` publishes them later (behind the session's drain, the lifecycle, or a full-hub `Pub snapshot` line, about 0.9 s), with no check that the report is still current. The esp-mqtt task still publishes its own reports at once: `rules_config` (`app_iothub.c:1616`), `set_hub_name` (1688) and `apply_twin_desired()` (1894, also from the twin GET response at every connect, 1944). So: a `hub_name` or `snapshot_interval_s` desired change during an outage, then a reconnect with a backlog (the GET response's fresh report goes first, then the session twin built before the drain); or a provision followed quickly by `rules_config` while `cloud_tx` prints a snapshot line (the provision's twin overwrites `auto_close_enabled` / `trigger_mask`). The reported twin then shows the old value until the next report; the firmware itself applies the right config. At `d0d5284` the window was milliseconds. **Options:** a fix of about 15 lines (the three esp-mqtt-task sites set a "twin owed" flag and wake `iothub_task`, which builds a fresh twin and posts it to the FIFO: one build site, one ordered sender), before CP6 or in WP3; or accept it as a residual. Bench check: 15k item 14 (TW-1).
4. **TC-4 / R14:** accept (fixer, safety, cloud).
5. **D2, recorded:** the asynchronous DPS registration is required before or with any `AZURE_DPS_PROV_EPOCH` bump.

**Residual risks** (none blocks WP2c; the owner is in brackets):
- **R1.** A live DPS registration on `iothub_task`, uncached hubs only (D2). [a later WP, before any epoch bump]
- **R2. stdout.** UART0 at 115,200 baud has no TX buffer: a leak whose rules lines (`rules_engine.c` 771, 852 at `d0d5284`) come while `cloud_tx` prints a full-hub `Pub snapshot` line waits up to about 0.9 s before the RMLEAK and CLOSE posts. `cloud_tx` can run that round at priority 5, inherited through esp-mqtt's API lock or the stdout lock, because FreeRTOS lowers an inherited priority only when the task holds no mutex and `cloud_tx` holds the busy mutex for the whole round; nothing that waits on `cloud_tx` is starved (two cores), and `d0d5284` printed the same lines from `iothub_task` at priority 5 (a T6-11 task dump may show `cloud_tx` at 5). [D4: CP6 item 12; WP3 if a CLOSE exceeds 200 ms]
- **R3. NVS and flash:** at most one NVS operation by another task, plus page-erase stalls of both cores (flash auto-suspend is off). When a stalled session dies, `cloud_tx` stores everything queued (up to 24 stores, each with a meta write and a commit) in one burst, so a leak landing then can wait behind an erase. [D4: CP6 item 12 records the store lines next to any slow CLOSE]
- **R4.** At least 24 items queued in one stall lose the overflow (E line); as few as 8 safety events with 16 health alerts queued (SAFE-1). Since WP2e at least 16 safety events, with at most 8 health alerts or twin reports queued (15p). [SAFE-1: done in WP2e; the overflow itself: WP9 if CP6's flood calls for it]
- **R5. The PUBACK window** (15i residual 7, unchanged): an event esp-mqtt took (msg_id ≥ 0) into a session that is dying silently is lost when the outbox expires it 30 s after its transmit (resends do not refresh that tick; IDF 5.5.1 `mqtt_client.c:2247`, 1546-1565, 1652); A-1 covers only a publish that sees the session's end. On CP6 item 12 the wets at +1 s and +5 s of the outage are likely to be this (their writes fit in the TCP send buffer). The proposal's "closed: the loss on outbox expiry at the end of a session" holds only for the publish that observes the end. [a later release: keep each event until `MQTT_EVENT_PUBLISHED`]
- **R6.** More QoS 1 duplicates, possibly after newer events (D3; the cloud dedupes on the identical payload, TC-2 decided). Outages under 30 s can also invert the first copies, as at `d0d5284`: on reconnect esp-mqtt resends its QUEUED items (an A-1'd write that returned −1) before its TRANSMITTED ones (`mqtt_client.c:1738-1762`). [the cloud: order by `ts`]
- **R7.** Stack sizes rest on estimates until T6-11. [CP6 item 16; WP9's trim]
- **R8. Health alerts** can be delayed for the length of a stall (lossless; `ts` is the build time). While one is parked and the health engine's 8-deep queue is full, the engine keeps the old rating and prints `ALERT QUEUE FULL — … held for retry` at every evaluation (`health_engine.c:626`), so ratings and the fleet LED keep their state until TX is idle: errs safe, and the rules engine does not read ratings. A parked alert for a device removed meanwhile can arrive after the removal's cmd_ack (nothing purges it). [the cloud ignores health events for device ids it no longer has; or WP9/WP10 drops parked alerts for removed ids]
- **R9.** The asynchronous snapshot bookkeeping is new logic. Under steady event traffic (a provision pulse with many sensors, a chatty LoRa sensor) the flush can be deferred pass after pass, each deferral bounded by `cloud_tx`'s idle wake or the 2 s poll. [CP6 items 14 and 15]
- **R10.** The decommission park, up to 25 s plus 3 s, on a hub that is already empty (`Rx Queue Full` from the sensors just removed is expected then).
- **R11.** `Pub` and buffer lines print on `cloud_tx`, so their place among `iothub_task`'s lines changes. `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` 0.14 now says so; tests that match lines by adjacency are rewritten in WP10. [WP10]
- **R12.** Pre-existing: the `a` key toggles the LoRa ACKs in production builds. [WP10 register]
- **R13.** `rules_engine_evaluate_leak` failed open on a lock timeout: **closed by WP2d** (15o).
- **R14.** TC-4 (above). [accepted]
- **R15. CONC-1's remainder:** a device-set change that lands while the snapshot's write waits for esp-mqtt's API lock is still published after the ack. It is detected (`SNAP published while the device set changed - the reconciled one follows now`) and followed at once by the reconciled snapshot. An OK_LATE can also follow a change that landed just after a write that went out first: one extra, already reconciled snapshot, which skips the 5 s minimum interval once. [none: nothing outside esp-mqtt can close that wait]
- **R16. TW-1** (above). **Fixed in WP2e** (15p); what remains is esp-mqtt's QoS 1 resend of an older report (15p W1, pre-existing). [the user, 15p]
- **R17. `wifi_task` while a stop waits:** it now returns at once and runs `ap_tail_maintain()` and `router_retry()` while the stop is still pending behind `cloud_tx`'s publish (at most one publish: its `Pub` line plus a write of up to about 10 s, about 20 s behind esp-mqtt's own write). Likely an improvement on WP2b's block, but in the top-priority portal-bug area. `MQTT client stopped on wifi_task in N.N s` now counts from the try-take that succeeded, so it excludes the wait for the gate. [CP6 item 13]
- **R18.** Pre-existing, less likely since WP2c: `iothub_mqtt_stop_service()` ignores `esp_mqtt_client_stop()`'s result, so a failed DISCONNECT write (ESP_FAIL with the client still running) is marked stopped. A `cloud_tx` write that fails now ends the session first, and the stop then skips the DISCONNECT. [WP3 or WP10, with 15m residual 3] *Closed by WP3 (15q): the stop's result is checked and a refusal retried; R18's own case (a failed DISCONNECT write) cannot happen in IDF 5.5.1, where `send_disconnect_msg()` returns `ESP_OK`.*
- **R19. LoRa ACKs behind stdout:** `lora_task` prints `Packet Received` before `send_ack()` and the `lora_rx_queue` post (`app_lora.cpp:287-305` at `d0d5284`); while `cloud_tx` prints a long line the ACK can miss the sensor's receive window and the packet reaches `iothub_task` late. The bench's leak-to-CLOSE metric, timed from the sensor line, does not show it. [CP6 item 12: watch for repeated LoRa packets and `Rx Queue Full`]
- **Comment nits** [WP10 register]: "cloud_tx takes no app mutex" should except `net_status`'s, reached through the DISCONNECTED dispatch (held only for the LED update); the inherited priority (R2); `app_wifi.c:1385-1391` does not say that a stop found behind a `cloud_tx` publish runs on a later pass (`app_iothub.h:68-75` does); `telemetry_v2.c:204-206` says the gate covers "this one write", while the `Pub` line also prints under it (about 0.9 s for a full hub's snapshot).

**Closed by WP2c:** LS-1 (WP2b-LS-1, 15m's decision item 2); 15i residuals 8, 9 (for events published after the link-loss mark) and 11; 15m residual 8; F-08's reconnect-pass gap; the loss on outbox expiry for a publish that observes its session's end.

**Documents still to follow** (WP10 register): `docs/telemetry/telemetry_messages.md` is generated from `messages_data.py`, and neither has the delivery note yet (`C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 has it); `TELEMETRY_REFERENCE.md` (firmware 1.8.0, superseded) still names `iothub_task` as the publisher and gives an older dedupe tuple; `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md` tests that read a `Pub` line next to `iothub_task`'s lines (T5-03, T5-12, the DEC tests) are covered by the 0.14 note until WP10 rewrites them; `docs/telemetry/validate_capture.py` checks order in arrival order with no dedupe, so a late A-1 duplicate can be flagged (the test plan's header note says to classify it with the UART log; WP10: dedupe by identical body before the ordering checks); the test plan's header table and VAL-01 still carry earlier checkpoints' commits and figures (15j has CP6's).

### 15o. WP2d: a busy lock no longer drops a wet report (2026-10-02)

**Why** (D5, the user's decision of 2026-10-02). Pre-existing, found while writing WP2c (its §3.1 correction): inside `rules_engine_evaluate_leak()`, a 1 s timeout on the rules engine's own mutex or on the provisioning mutex returned **without latching or closing** (`rules_engine.c:722-725, 737-746` at `d0d5284`), and the BLE scanner's delta gate may not send the same wet state again for up to 5 min. WP2c added no contender for those mutexes, but they can be held for a second or more: a C2D handler's NVS work, a provisioning save. The user decided a package of its own right after WP2c, with its own review, built into CP6 if the review ended in time (D7): it did.

**Commits** on `fix/2.1.4` after `4ed8c77`: two implementer commits and six fixer commits (the review's ten real findings). Each compiled on its own in the scratchpad with the project's flags, with no warning; every file that includes the changed headers compiles at HEAD. Nothing pushed, amended or rebased; `sdkconfig`, NVS keys and `build\` untouched; CRLF kept. **`fcc0979` was the last commit that touched firmware sources until WP2e; CP6 is now of `545b8f2`** (15j, 15p).

| SHA | Finding | What |
|---|---|---|
| `dd4eb0f` | D5 | `provisioning_get_rules_and_state()` reads the state and the rules in one hold (silent; false only on a NULL argument, before init, or a 1 s timeout). The rules engine keeps copies: the last device set it applied (`g_last_set`, about 376 B), written only on `iothub_task` (init and each device-set change), and the last rules read (`g_last_rules`, default enabled / all triggers), written under its mutex by every live read and by `rules_config`. On a busy provisioning read, the leak is decided on the copies: a sensor in the last set latches and closes, any other is ignored. On a busy rules lock, a wet report from a sensor in the last set is kept (up to 4) and evaluated again on a later pass, with `iothub_task` polling every 100 ms while any is kept. `rules_engine_has_kept_reports()` |
| `564b544` | same class | `rules_live_or_last()` (the live rules, or the copy on a timeout) at three sites that took a timeout as "auto-close off": `override_cancel` (it fell into the "no active leaks" cleanup, wiping the latch with no close), the valve reconnect's Priority 1 (it fell into Priority 2, which with the valve open and RMLEAK clear infers a physical override and blocks auto-close for 24 h), and the override's expiry in the tick (the window ended and the close was skipped, with nothing to retry it) |
| `061c126` | review F4 = WP2D-C2 | A kept entry carries its wet or dry state: a dry report meeting a busy lock is kept behind its source's kept wet one, so wet then dry replays in order; a repeat of its source's last kept state is folded into it |
| `95b91b0` | review F2, F3, WP2D-C4 wording | The retry runs after the tick and the loop's take of the tick's event (`rules_engine_retry_kept_reports()`, followed by a second take), and stops while an event is still untaken, so a kept report's `auto_close` never replaces the C2D event that caused the keep (`auto_close_reenabled`, `rmleak_cleared`, `water_access_override_enabled`). Only on a pass whose tick took the rules lock. The tick no longer returns early: only its auto-clear (Check 1) waits while reports are kept; the override expiry and Check 2 run. A report from a source with one kept waits behind it without trying the lock |
| `077b299` | review F1 = WP2D-C1 | `rules_engine_on_valve_replaced()` drops the kept reports of the valve source, before its lock take, so also when that take times out |
| `ab82c6a` | review F5 (pre-existing) | A wet source is tracked only once it is decided this hub's (live read or the copy); a dry report still untracks first. A live read that finds the hub unprovisioned no longer tracks the source |
| `a71269b` | review WP2D-C3 | On the busy-provisioning fallback, `auto_close` and `auto_close_blocked_override` name the valve from the copy (`g_last_set.valve_mac`, upper case like the live read), not from a third 1 s read |
| `fcc0979` | review F6, WP2D-C5 | `rules_engine_init()` returns whether its reads worked; if not, `iothub_task` raises a device-set change and the loop re-reads both copies until it succeeds (W at boot). A W line says when the nested rules read timed out in a device-set change (retry unchanged) |

**What the image does differently** (only when a lock is busy; with both free the decisions, lines and order are `d0d5284`'s, with one provisioning hold instead of two and a 2-byte copy store)
- **Provisioning busy for 1 s at a leak:** the membership gate's 1 s wait gives UNKNOWN (counted as a member, as before), then the rules read times out after 1 s. The leak is decided on the copies: W `Provisioning busy for 1 s - leak from %s sensor %s decided on the last known devices and rules (provisioned here)` (or `(not provisioned here, ignored)`, `(no device list yet, ignored)`), then for a member the latch, RMLEAK and CLOSE as usual, about 2 s after the report (up to 1 s more for a valve-flood source). The `auto_close` names the valve from the copy. Before WP2d the report was dropped.
- **Rules lock busy for 1 s:** a report from a sensor in the last applied set is kept, W `Rules lock busy for 1 s - wet report from %s sensor %s kept, evaluated again next pass` (once per episode); Phase 3 still sends `leak_detected` at once. `iothub_task` then wakes every 100 ms; on each pass whose tick took the lock it evaluates the kept reports oldest first, after the tick's own event was taken, stopping while an event is untaken. So `auto_close` follows `leak_detected` and any C2D event built meanwhile, and the CLOSE follows the lock's release within about one 100 ms pass (one more per earlier kept report that built an event). A full list (4): E `Rules lock busy - %d leak reports already kept, the %s one from %s sensor %s is lost`. A sensor outside the set, or a dry report with nothing kept for its source, gets the old `Failed to take mutex` and is dropped as before (a dry report's next resend repairs it). While reports are kept, the tick's auto-clear waits.
- **The three sites of `564b544`** keep closing tracked leaks on a busy provisioning read (above).
- **Boot:** a failed rules-engine read at init prints W `Boot: rules engine missed the device list or rules - reading them again in the loop`, then a device-set change re-reads them; until then the copies are empty (a busy-fallback leak is then ignored, `no device list yet`) and the rules copy is enabled / all triggers (protective).
- **`rules_config`** also writes the rules copy under the rules lock (up to 5 s on the esp-mqtt task, as `rules_engine_reset_all()`); W `Rules lock busy - the copy used while provisioning is busy keeps the old rules` if it cannot.

WP2d grew from the planned 30-40 lines (synthesis D5) to about 390 changed lines in five files (`rules_engine.c/.h`, `provisioning_manager.c/.h`, `app_iothub.c`), about half of them comments: CP6's reviewers should size it so.

**Log lines** (diffed against `4ed8c77`; the production tool's version, `App version`, Gateway ID, Wi-Fi MAC, LoRa and NimBLE patterns match none; no credential):
- New, `RULES_ENGINE`: W `Provisioning busy for 1 s - leak from %s sensor %s decided on the last known devices and rules (%s)`; W `Rules lock busy for 1 s - wet report from %s sensor %s kept, evaluated again next pass`; E `Rules lock busy - %d leak reports already kept, the %s one from %s sensor %s is lost`; W `Rules lock busy - the copy used while provisioning is busy keeps the old rules`; W `Provisioning busy for 1 s - the rules copy was not refreshed, the device-set change is retried`.
- New, `IOTHUB`: W `Boot: rules engine missed the device list or rules - reading them again in the loop`.
- Same text, new trigger: `RULES_ENGINE: Failed to take mutex` now prints only for a report that is dropped (a dry one with nothing kept for its source, or one from a sensor outside the set); `RULES_ENGINE: Valve replaced: old valve leak source %s, …` says dropped also when only a kept report was removed; `RULES_ENGINE: No valve identity available — event emitted without valve_id` no longer prints on the busy fallback; `PROVISIONING: Failed to take mutex in is_provisioned` and `… in get_rules_config` no longer print from a leak evaluation (the new read is silent), but still from the three `564b544` sites (`get_rules_config`).
- Removed: none.

**Memory** (objects at `-Og`, `4ed8c77` against `fcc0979`): `rules_engine.c` `.text` +1,701 B, `.rodata` +609 B, `.data` +2 B (the rules copy's default), `.bss` +477 B (the device-set copy, the kept list of 4 × 20 B, flags); `provisioning_manager.c` `.text` +135 B; `app_iothub.c` `.text` +115 B, `.rodata` +100 B. **Total: `.text` +1,951 B, `.rodata` +709 B, `.data` +2 B, `.bss` +477 B.** IRAM 0: no `IRAM_ATTR`, no `.iram` section; the new references are `memmove` (ROM, 0x40001200), `snprintf` (flash, linked), and the three new flash functions `provisioning_get_rules_and_state`, `rules_engine_has_kept_reports` and `rules_engine_retry_kept_reports`. Stack: the evaluate path +96 B (`evaluate_leak_locked` 96 B); the retry chain adds 144 B under the tick's 64 B; `forget_unprovisioned` stays 432 B; `iothub_task`'s frame stays 624 B. `sdkconfig`, NVS keys and partitions unchanged.

**The `.bss` gate** (plan §10, ≤ +450 B through WP8 against `520b17a`): WP0 +121, WP1 −38, WP2 +86, WP2b −75, WP2d +477 = **+571 B without WP2c's approved +6,301 B**, 121 B over the gate, and WP3-WP8 still plan about +340 B of their own. For the user (below). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: WP2d's +477 B is recorded under the D5 approval, like WP2c's +6,301 B under D1** (it is the fail-safe copy of the device set). The gate then counts WP0 +121, WP1 −38, WP2 +86, WP2b −75 and WP2e +29 (15p) = **+123 B**, which leaves 327 B for WP3-WP8's planned ≈ +340 B: about 13 B short, for WP9 (15p).

**The council** (safety, rtos, cloud; on `dd4eb0f` … `fcc0979`): **3/3 SHIP, no blocking issue.** Checked and passed: every `evaluate_leak_locked()` return gives the rules mutex; the kept replay runs the same post-decision code, so RMLEAK before CLOSE, the latch, the override block, the cooldown and the settle barrier are unchanged; no new lock-order edge (the rules → provisioning order already existed, and provisioning never calls into the rules or health engines while it holds its mutex; its only callbacks take `s_mac_lock` and the command queue); the unlocked statics are touched only on `iothub_task`, checked against every call site; the rules copy is always written under the rules mutex, after the provisioning write, so it cannot go stale backwards; no new foreign-sensor close (a report is kept only for a sensor in the last set, checked again at the retry; the only foreign path left is the pre-existing gate-UNKNOWN plus a free live read, N4); the busy path's `valve_id` is upper case, and a copy with no valve skips `auto_close` as the live path does; F-08's single slot, cause before consequence, event `ts` at the build, the lifecycle and twin cadence and `telemetry_v2_is_connected()` unchanged; with nothing kept and provisioning free, behaviour is `d0d5284`'s; scratch compiles clean, no IRAM, no new library symbol but `memmove` (ROM); every subject 57-67 characters with the exact trailer.

**For the user's decision**
1. **A dry valve flood report is dropped on a busy rules lock when its wet report was tracked, not kept** (pre-existing). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: for WP3, keep dry valve-flood reports on a busy rules lock** (the WP3 row of §15's table). "valve" then stays wet until the valve's next link-up, which in the field can be days: `leak_reset` stays refused, and each override expiry closes the valve again. It fails safe. With the keep machinery in place, keeping dry valve-source reports unconditionally is cheap; the safety specialist recommends it for WP3. *Done in WP3 (15q): `1afb368`.*
2. **WP2D-C4, the exact fix:** let the live provisioning read decide membership in the same hold (a change to `provisioning_get_rules_and_state()`). **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: for WP3** (the WP3 row of §15's table). It closes the removal window below and the pre-existing gate-UNKNOWN hub-level check (N4). The rtos specialist recommends it for WP3; safety and cloud find deferring fine. *Done in WP3 (15q): `1afb368`.*
3. **Accepted costs of the design** (fixer): a kept report waits one more pass (about 100 ms) when an event is still untaken, and so does each further kept report after one that built an event; a wet report arriving while others are kept waits for the next pass even with the lock free; Phase 2's new reports can still overwrite a C2D event built while they waited on the lock (pre-existing).
4. **The `.bss` gate:** record WP2d's +477 B as part of the D5 approval (it is the fail-safe copy of the device set), or have WP9 recover it. **Decided by the user on 2026-10-02: recorded under the D5 approval** (above; `RADIO_PORTAL_PLAN.md` §10's note). Either way plan §10's ≤ +450 B (without WP2c) is already exceeded by 121 B before WP3-WP8.

**Residual risks** (none blocks; each needs coincident rare conditions and fails toward closing, or is log-only; owner in brackets):
1. **The WP2D-C4 window is wider than the "≤ 100 ms" in the comments** (rtos). The retry runs after the select wait (≤ 100 ms), the tick (≤ 100 ms) and the loop's take (≤ 1 s), and on every pass while a device-set apply is deferred (`app_iothub.c` returns before `forget_unprovisioned()` and `sync_valve_detectors()` when provisioning or health is busy), judging kept reports on the stale copy; `077b299`'s drop of kept valve reports at a swap does not run then either. The same when `sync_valve_detectors()`'s own provisioning read times out right after `forget_unprovisioned()` has loaded the new valve (safety, cloud). An old valve's kept flood report can then pend RMLEAK and CLOSE for the new valve (E-04 class), usually withdrawn by the next `on_valve_replaced()` before the new valve links; the `auto_close` naming the new valve stays on the wire. Hardening: skip the retry while a device-set change is pending, or keep the valve MAC in the kept entry and compare it at the retry. [WP3: with the decided WP2D-C4 fix, item 2 above]
2. **A flapping source can fill the 4-entry list** (W, D, W, D): the next W is lost with the E line, and the engine ends dry while the source is wet; the first W has already latched and closed, but the all-clear then clears RMLEAK after 10 s, and the valve source does not announce itself again. Needs 5 edges inside one busy hold. Hardening: when the list is full and the source has kept entries, overwrite its last entry's state. [WP3] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `8680b32`, `e1ae26d`, `bd575fe`; a wet report never gives way to a dry one, and each source keeps at most one report of each state.*
3. **Pre-existing:** `rules_engine_on_valve_replaced()` returns on a 1 s rules-lock timeout while `sync_valve_detectors()` commits the new MAC anyway, so the swap's purge is never retried and the old valve's tracked flood source survives (E-04). [WP3: a retry flag] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `8680b32`, `f639297`; the purge is owed and runs first, once, in the next rules-lock hold on any task.*
4. **A Priority-2 override inference racing a kept wet report:** needs the valve offline for the whole incident, its pended close cancelled by a dry report, a re-wet during a busy episode and a reconnect on that very pass. Do not "fix" it by skipping the inference while reports are kept: that undoes a real button press (F3). [none]
5. **Boot window:** if init's rules read fails but its device-set read works, the copy is enabled / all triggers, so a monitor-only hub deciding on the busy fallback could close before the first successful re-read (protective; `fcc0979` shortens it). The same after a 5 s rules-lock failure on `rules_config`, until the next live read. [none]
6. **Log only:** the once-per-episode flag of the `Rules lock busy for 1 s` line is not reset when `on_valve_replaced()` empties the kept list, so the next episode's W line can be missing (fix: reset it when the count reaches 0). [WP10 register] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `8680b32`.*
7. While reports are kept, a busy nested rules read in `forget_unprovisioned()` re-raises the device-set change on every 100 ms pass (about 1.1 s with the 1 s wait), so one twin per pass goes out while provisioning stays busy (twin-version churn; WP2D-C5 says it was every 2 s). [doc]
8. **`rules_config`'s cmd_ack** can now wait up to 5 s for the rules lock on the esp-mqtt task (before WP2d it did not take it); in practice about 1 s or less. [CP6 item 17]
9. **Cloud:** a kept report's `auto_close` can arrive after the same sensor's `leak_cleared` (the lock busy across two passes: wet kept, `leak_detected` sent; dry kept, `leak_cleared` sent; the wet retried on the third pass); two `auto_close` for one sensor in one second (a retry and a `cause:"reconnect"`) share the D3 key (15n TC-2); under the identical-payload rule the user chose on 2026-10-02 they differ in `data.cause`, so the cloud keeps both. The cloud correlates `auto_close` by device and `ts`, never by adjacency (`C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5). [the cloud]
10. **Not reached on a normal bench:** the kept and busy paths need a lock held 1 s or more; without the hold image of 15k item 17 they are reviewed, not benched. [CP6 item 17]
11. Pre-existing single pending slot: a C2D event built between the retry's give and the loop's second take can replace the kept report's `auto_close` (a window of microseconds, Phase 2's class). [none]

**Other leak-path early returns on a lock timeout** (found by the implementer; reported, not fixed): (1) the valve's identity in `app_iothub.c`: if `provisioning_get_valve_mac()` is busy with no `s_det_valve_mac` fallback or no valve target, `Valve identity unavailable` and the valve's flood report is never evaluated (the valve does not re-notify a steady flood); (2) `rules_engine_on_valve_connected()`: a 1 s rules-lock timeout returns silently, so that reconnect's reconciliation is lost until the next CONNECTED (pended RMLEAK and CLOSE still apply at setup); (3) `rules_engine_take_pending_telemetry()`: a 1 s timeout returns NULL, and the event can be overwritten in the single slot (F-08's class, not a lost close); (4) `ble_valve_set_rmleak()` / `ble_valve_close()`: a full `ble_cmd_queue` (10 ms post) logs `enqueue FAILED` and loses the command (not a lock); (5) `rules_engine_handle_config_command()`: a provisioning timeout merges into the defaults (on / all; the config path); (6) delays only: `add_device_id()`'s valve read, `sensor_meta_get()` and `valve_provisioned_or_unknown()` on the live path, each up to 1 s under the rules lock before the posts; (7) the tick's 100 ms take: retried next pass. [WP3 register]

**Docs:** `dd4eb0f`'s message body is out of date in three places: it says the retry runs first in the tick (since `95b91b0` it runs after the tick and its event's take), that a dry report still drops (since `061c126` it is kept behind its source's kept wet one), and that a kept report is dropped once its sensor has left the set, without the WP2D-C4 qualification (a removal in the window before the retry is judged on the hub's state).

### 15p. WP2e: every twin report built on `iothub_task` and sent in order; 8 deferrable items; the dedupe rule (2026-10-02)

**Why: the user's decisions of 2026-10-02** on 15n's "For the user's decision" items 1-3 (the WP2c design they amend is in the session scratchpad, `…\scratchpad\council\wp2c\WP2C_PROPOSAL.md`; restated in 15n):
1. **TW-1, fixed before CP6.** Every twin-reported write is built fresh on `iothub_task` and sent in order through `cloud_tx`'s FIFO, so the last report written is always current. The three esp-mqtt-task report sites stop publishing: they set a "twin owed" request (one writer per variable) and wake `iothub_task`, which builds a fresh twin and posts it (one build site, one ordered sender). A twin built earlier and still queued must not overwrite a newer one. The twin is still reported at every device-set change and every CONNECTED, only while connected, and the cmd_ack contract for `rules_config` and `set_hub_name` is kept.
2. **SAFE-1: `TX_HEALTH_MAX_QUEUED` 16 → 8,** so at least 16 of the FIFO's 24 slots are always left for safety events.
3. **TC-2: dedupe on the identical payload.** The cloud drops a message only if it is byte-identical to one already received (a payload hash, for example); the A-1 comment says so.

Also decided on 2026-10-02 (15o): WP2d's `.bss` +477 B is recorded under D5's approval, and WP3 gains two items, dry valve-flood reports kept on a busy rules lock and membership decided in the same provisioning hold (WP2D-C4; the WP3 row of §15's table).

**Commits** on `fix/2.1.4` after `6d8f7ec`: three implementer commits and three review fixes. The review's ten findings were five issues, each reported twice: three defects, fixed by the fixer; one documentation item (TW1-R5 = TW1-F5, recorded below); one for the user (TW1-R2 = TW1-F4, W1 below). Each commit compiled on its own in the scratchpad (`…\scratchpad\council\wp2e\`) with the project's `compile_commands.json` flags, objects outside `build\`, with no warning; `app_iothub.c`, `telemetry_v2.c` and `health_engine.c` (every file that includes `telemetry_v2.h`) compile at HEAD. Nothing pushed, amended or rebased; `sdkconfig`, NVS keys, partitions and `build\` untouched; line endings kept. **`545b8f2` is the last commit that touches firmware sources, and CP6 is of it** (15j). WP2e adds 221 lines and removes 64 in three files against `6d8f7ec`, much of it comments.

| SHA | Finding | What |
|---|---|---|
| `2ef2b58` | TW-1 (decision 1) | The three esp-mqtt-task sites, `rules_config` (`app_iothub.c:1621`), `set_hub_name` (:1693) and `apply_twin_desired()` (:1965, also reached from the twin GET's answer, :2015), call `twin_request()`: it counts a request (`s_twin_req`, that task its only writer) and wakes `iothub_task`. One build, `twin_build()`, on `iothub_task`, for the session twin (`post_session_twin()`, the session queue) and for `post_twin_reported()` (the FIFO: a device-set change's report, or an owed one from the loop's session block). Each report carries its build number in the FIFO item's two spare bytes (`telem_tx_item_t.seq`; still 12 B, the `_Static_assert` holds), and `cloud_tx_twin()` skips one older than a report already handled. A FIFO with no room leaves the report owed, polled every 2 s, instead of losing it. `publish_twin_reported()` is removed |
| `c71249e` | SAFE-1 (decision 2) | `TX_HEALTH_MAX_QUEUED` 16 → 8, and the three comments that state it |
| `34f3825` | TC-2 (decision 3) | The A-1 comment in `send_str()` (`telemetry_v2.c:346-352`): the cloud drops a message only when it is byte-identical to one it already has, and A-1's copy is byte-identical (it is the published text, and `ob_prepare_replay_locked()` publishes a synced entry unchanged, `offline_buffer.c:315-316`). Comment only: the object's code is identical |
| `0bbbef0` | review TW1-R1 = TW1-F2 | A report esp-mqtt refuses with its session still up (−2 at the 12,288 B outbox limit, −1 for no memory): `cloud_tx` counts it (`s_tx_twin_refused`), prints a W line and wakes `iothub_task`, which makes it owed and builds a fresh one no sooner than 5 s later (`TWIN_REPORT_RETRY_MS`; a request made meanwhile waits with it). A failed write ends the session and is left to the next CONNECTED. Before, a refused report was never sent again (the esp-mqtt task's direct reports had the same gap before WP2e) |
| `63c2252` | review TW1-R3 = TW1-F1 | `TELEM_TX_DEFERRABLE_MAX` (8) in `telemetry_v2.h` replaces `TX_HEALTH_MAX_QUEUED`. `post_twin_reported()` builds and posts only while fewer than 8 items wait, like a health alert, so at most 8 queued items are ever health alerts, twin reports or the snapshot, and at least 16 slots stay for leak, valve and rules events. Before, an owed twin, built in the session block ahead of the pass's events, could take the slot a `leak_detected` needed |
| `545b8f2` | review TW1-R4 = TW1-F3 | The staleness check compares with the newest report `cloud_tx` has seen (taken, refused or dropped at the session check), not the last one esp-mqtt took, and only a posted report uses a build number. The gap is then bounded by the reports still queued, so the 16-bit comparison cannot wrap (it could after 32,768 untaken builds, after which every newer report was skipped) |

**How it works (at `545b8f2`)**
- **Owed.** A report is owed while `s_twin_req != s_twin_req_done` (a request not yet covered) or `s_twin_due` is set (a device-set change's report that met 8 or more queued items or no memory, or a refusal). `twin_build()` reads `s_twin_req` before it builds, so a report covers every request made before it; `twin_posted()` then records that count and the build number, and clears `s_twin_due`. One writer each: `s_twin_req` the esp-mqtt task; `s_tx_twin_seq` and `s_tx_twin_refused` `cloud_tx`; `s_twin_req_done`, `s_twin_due`, `s_twin_seq`, `s_twin_refused_seen` and `s_twin_retry_ms` `iothub_task`.
- **Where reports are built**, all on `iothub_task`: at a CONNECTED, the session's report into the session queue, after the lifecycle (it covers whatever is owed); at a device-set change, at the loop top (`apply_device_set_change()`), into the FIFO; and an owed one in the session block, while connected, after the session's own, once fewer than 8 items wait and no sooner than 5 s after a refusal. While one is owed, the loop polls every 2 s. Offline nothing is built: the next CONNECTED's report covers it.
- **Staleness: a build number, not coalescing.** `cloud_tx_twin()` records the newest number seen, checks the session (`pub_begin_for()`: a report for an ended session is dropped silently, as before), then skips a report not newer than one already seen (I line). The wire never goes back, every report that is the newest so far is still sent (so "a report at every CONNECTED" and the order replay → lifecycle → twin → live are unchanged), and no report's fate depends on a later one getting through. Coalescing (dropping a queued report when a newer one is posted) would drop the session report whenever the GET's answer lands during a replay, moving the twin behind live events. Within a session every report is built in order on one task, so the only reorder left is between the queues: the session queue is served before the FIFO, so a FIFO report built before a session report can reach `cloud_tx` after it. That is when the I line prints.
- **cmd_ack, then twin, then snapshot, for every command.** esp-mqtt runs the C2D handler on its own task holding its API lock (`MQTT_API_LOCK`, IDF 5.5.1 `mqtt_client.c:1649-1817`), and the handler publishes the cmd_ack inside it (`app_iothub.c:1705`); `cloud_tx`'s publish of the report needs the same lock (`mqtt_client.c:2161`); the snapshot is built only into an idle TX. Before WP2e `rules_config` and `set_hub_name` wrote their twin before the ack. The documented contract, "republished immediately after the ack" (`docs/telemetry/c2d_commands_catalogue.md:52`) and the ack, twin, snapshot order of `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §4.9, is now met. Keeping twin-before-ack would need the esp-mqtt task to publish the twin itself (decided against), and waiting on `cloud_tx` inside the handler would deadlock on that lock. **On the UART `Twin reported` can still print first:** `twin_request()` (:1621) runs before the ack (:1705), and the line prints before `cloud_tx` waits for the lock. Only the wire order is guaranteed; `2ef2b58`'s message, which says the line prints after the ack's `Pub` line, is wrong there (review TW1-R5 = TW1-F5; no amend).
- **SAFE-1, for every item that can wait.** Health alerts (`telemetry_v2.c:1656`) and twin reports (`app_iothub.c:1908`) enter the FIFO one at a time, only while fewer than `TELEM_TX_DEFERRABLE_MAX` (8) items wait, and a snapshot only into an idle TX (`telemetry_v2.c:1642`): at most 8 queued items are of the kinds that can wait without loss, and at least 16 slots stay for leak, valve and rules events. Only the decommission's FINAL snapshot and clear are posted regardless, on a hub that is then empty. A decommission-all's twin that meets 8 or more is skipped; the connect after the restart reports it.

**Every twin report, before and after**

| # | Report | At `6d8f7ec` | At `545b8f2` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | CONNECTED (every session) | Built on `iothub_task` in the session block; the session queue; sent by `cloud_tx` after the replay and the lifecycle | Unchanged; numbered, and it covers whatever is owed |
| 2 | Device-set change (`provision`, `decommission` valve, lora, ble or all, and `iothub_task`'s own retries) | Built on `iothub_task` at the loop top; the FIFO; lost on a full FIFO (`TX queue full (24) - twin not sent`) | Unchanged, numbered; posted only while fewer than 8 items wait, else owed and built fresh later. Order: the ack (esp-mqtt task), the twin, the snapshot. Decommission-all: twin, FINAL snapshot, clear (L17), its twin skipped with 8 or more queued |
| 3 | `rules_config` | Built and published by the esp-mqtt task, before its ack | A request; `iothub_task` builds it on its next pass; `cloud_tx` sends it through the FIFO, after the ack |
| 4 | `set_hub_name` | As 3 | As 3 |
| 5 | A desired-properties PATCH | Built and published at once by the esp-mqtt task (no ack exists) | A request, then as 3 |
| 6 | The twin GET's answer, at every connect | As 5 | As 5 |
| 7 | An owed report (new) | – | Built in `iothub_task`'s session block into the FIFO while connected: a request not yet covered, a device-set report that met 8 or more queued items, or a refusal (5 s later) |

The twin GET itself (a request, not a report) is unchanged: the esp-mqtt task sends it at the SUBACK, and `cloud_tx` retries it every 5 s.

**Traces** (the implementer's)
- **`rules_config`, then `provision` quickly.** If `iothub_task` runs between them, its session block builds report n (the new rules) into the FIFO, and the next pass's device-set change builds n+1 (both changes); if it runs after both, the loop-top change builds n, which covers the request, and nothing else is owed: one report. Either way the last report is current. The order TW-1 was found with (a provision, then `rules_config` while a snapshot line prints) now gives the provision's n and then n+1 with the new rules, last; before, the queued provision report overwrote the direct `rules_config` one.
- **A desired `hub_name` changed during an outage, then a reconnect with a backlog.** At the CONNECTED `iothub_task` builds the lifecycle and report n (old name) into the session queue; `cloud_tx` prints `Draining …`, the `Replayed` lines, `Pub lifecycle` and `Twin reported` (n). After the SUBACK the esp-mqtt task sends the GET, applies its answer (`Twin: hub_name = 'NEW'`) and asks for a report; `iothub_task` builds n+1 (new name) into the FIFO; `cloud_tx` prints `Twin reported` (n+1). If the answer lands during the replay, the session queue still goes first (n), then the FIFO (n+1). If the session's report is built after the answer, n already carries the new name and covers the request: one report. Before, the GET's direct report went first and the session's older one last.
- **A device-set change during a stall.** Posted if fewer than 8 wait, else owed (the 2 s poll, no line); the leak path is untouched. If the stalled write fails, the session ends, n is dropped at the session check, and the next CONNECTED's report, built from the current set, covers it. A FIFO report built on the pass that first sees a new session (the loop-top change runs before the session block) is overtaken by that session's own report: I `Twin report n not sent - report n+1, built after it, went first`.

**Log lines** (every format string diffed against `6d8f7ec`; no line removed or reworded; `functional_test.py`'s version, `App version`, Gateway ID, Wi-Fi MAC and `BATT` patterns match none of the new lines; none logs a credential; both new lines are within `CONFIG_LOG_MAXIMUM_LEVEL=3`):
- New, `IOTHUB`, both from `cloud_tx`: I `Twin report %u not sent - report %u, built after it, went first`; its second number is the newest report seen, which may itself have been dropped or refused, so read "went first" as "was seen first". W `Twin report %u not taken by MQTT (msg_id=%d) - built again after %d s`, printed `… built again after 5 s`; at the 2 s poll the rebuild lands about 5-7 s later.
- Same text, new task: `IOTHUB: Twin reported (%d): %s` still prints for every report published, retries included, and now always from `cloud_tx` (for `rules_config`, `set_hub_name`, desired patches and the GET's answer it printed from the esp-mqtt task, before the ack). It prints before `cloud_tx` waits for esp-mqtt's lock, so it can come before the cmd_ack's `Pub event` line.
- No longer prints: `TELEMETRY_V2: TX queue full (24) - twin not sent` (a twin waits for room).
- Same text, sooner: `IOTHUB: health alerts held - TX busy, internal free %u B, largest %u B` from 8 queued items (16 before).

**Memory** (objects at `-Og` with the project's flags, `6d8f7ec` against `545b8f2`; CP6 measures the link):

| Object | `.text` | `.rodata` | `.bss` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app_iothub.c` | +625 B | +163 B | +29 B |
| `telemetry_v2.c` | +8 B | 0 | 0 |
| `health_engine.c` | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **Total** | **+633 B** | **+163 B** | **+29 B** |

- By commit (`app_iothub.c` `.text`): `2ef2b58` +362 B, `0bbbef0` +262 B, `63c2252` −4 B, `545b8f2` +5 B; `telemetry_v2.c`: `2ef2b58` +9 B, `c71249e` −1 B (the compare takes an immediate). `.rodata`: `2ef2b58` +77 B, `0bbbef0` +86 B. `.data` 0.

- **IRAM 0:** no `IRAM_ATTR`, no `.iram` section. All three objects reference exactly `6d8f7ec`'s undefined symbols and define the same globals (`nm -u`, `nm -g`), so nothing new is linked: DIRAM `.text` stays 113,387 B. WP2e's functions are static: 15j's third map search finds them by input section.
- **Stack** (`.su`): `cloud_tx_twin` 32 → 48 B (`cloud_tx`); `iothub_task`'s own frame unchanged at 624 B; its deepest twin path is 48 B deeper (`apply_device_set_change` 48 → `post_twin_reported` 64 → `twin_build` 32 → `build_twin_reported` 416, where `publish_twin_reported` 48 was), and `post_session_twin` 48 → 64 B. The esp-mqtt task no longer builds or publishes a twin (`build_twin_reported`'s 416 B frame and the send left it); `twin_request` is 32 B.
- **The `.bss` gate** (plan §10, ≤ +450 B through WP8 against `520b17a`): with WP2c's and WP2d's recorded under D1 and D5 (15o), WP0 +121, WP1 −38, WP2 +86, WP2b −75, WP2e +29 = **+123 B**. WP3-WP8 plan about +340 B, so about +463 B in all: 13 B over unless WP9 recovers some. [WP9]

**The council** (cloud, rtos, safety; on `2ef2b58` … `545b8f2`, against the IDF 5.5.1 esp-mqtt sources): **3/3 SHIP, no blocking issue.** Checked and passed:
- One writer per new variable, checked against the task of every call site; only `iothub_task` posts to the FIFO (`publish_json()` routes only its events to `post_event()`, `telemetry_v2.c:427`; every other `telemetry_v2_tx_post` caller is on it), so `post_twin_reported()`'s room check still holds at the post (rtos, safety).
- The build-number rule: `iothub_task` reads the session, then numbers the build, so numbers never decrease as sessions advance, and an ended session never connects again (`telemetry_v2_set_connected(true)` always bumps the generation). A report dropped at `pub_begin_for()` means every older one would be dropped too. Numbers are used only by posted reports, and every posted report reaches `cloud_tx_twin()` unless TX is frozen (only before the decommission restart), so the gap is at most 8 FIFO + 2 session + 1 in flight, far below 2^15 (cloud, rtos).
- Cadence: one session report per CONNECTED (outside the 8), plus the GET answer's, as before; one per device-set change; nothing built offline (`post_twin_reported()` returns without making a report owed while disconnected, and the next CONNECTED's report clears what is owed) (cloud).
- The cmd_ack order against IDF 5.5.1's `mqtt_client.c` (above). A failed write aborts the session and dispatches DISCONNECTED on `cloud_tx` before its publish returns (`mqtt_client.c:2215`, 838-849), so the refusal check (`app_iothub.c:3490`) never counts it; the −2 path is `outbox.limit` (`mqtt_client.c:2181-2184`) (cloud, rtos).
- No loop and no new wait: at most one refused report per 5 s plus one per CONNECTED; every new cross-task call has a 0 timeout (queue sends, the gate's try-take); the W line prints after the gate is given back; nothing new runs under esp-mqtt's API lock (rtos).
- SAFE-1 by induction over FIFO posts, with only the decommission's FINAL snapshot and clear exempt (above); the leak path is untouched: Phase 2, `rules_engine.c`, the scanners and the leak, valve and rules event builders have no change, and `telemetry_v2.c` changes only comments, the limit and `.seq = 0` initialisers (safety).
- TC-2's comment matches the code: the buffer refuses an entry over 512 B whole (`offline_buffer.c:183-189`) instead of truncating it, and the replay publishes a synced entry unchanged, so A-1's two copies are byte-identical (cloud).
- Independent scratch compiles at HEAD (`wp2e\cloud_chk`, and `wp2e\rtos_chk` without `-MD`/`-MF`, so nothing reached `build\`): no warning, objects byte-identical to the fixer's, the same undefined symbols, no IRAM section, frames unchanged; line endings kept; subjects 58-69 characters, each with the exact trailer (cloud, rtos).

**For the user's decision**
1. **W1 (review TW1-R2 = TW1-F4; pre-existing, not a TW-1 regression): an older twin report can still reach IoT Hub after a newer one.** Reports go at QoS 1, so esp-mqtt resends one not yet acknowledged after 1 s (`message_retransmit_timeout`, `mqtt_client.c:1756-1760`) and keeps it across its own reconnects for up to 30 s. On a slow link (a round trip over 1 s) or across a reconnect, an older report's resend can land after a newer report, and IoT Hub applies the later arrival; the value then stays until the next report. **(a)** Send reported PATCHes at QoS 0, with IoT Hub's `$iothub/twin/res/204/?$rid=` response (already subscribed, logged as `Twin response: … (0 bytes)`) as the acknowledgement, and a rebuild if none arrives within N s while connected. That removes the resends, and for twins the −2 outbox refusals (`outbox.limit` applies only to QoS > 0) and the 30 s resends across reconnects. A protocol change: confirm that IoT Hub accepts QoS 0 on that topic (the council believes it does, and that Azure's C SDK sends twin PATCHes at most once), then bench it. **(b)** Record it as a known limit. **The council recommends (b) for CP6** (recorded here, in `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 and in 15k item 14(g)) **and (a) for WP3.**

**For the app and cloud teams** (through the user)
- **The app:** a command's twin report now follows its cmd_ack. Its delay is the wake, the build and its place in the FIFO: normally under a second, in 2 s steps while 8 or more items wait, about 5-7 s after a refusal. An app that reads `reported` once on the ack can read the old value: wait for the reported-property change, or poll (`C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5).
- **The cloud, optional later:** a per-boot message counter in the envelope's `gateway` object (a later schema revision) would let the identical-payload dedupe tell apart two real messages that print identically within one second (W8); A-1 copies would inherit it.

**Residual risks** (none blocks CP6; owner in brackets)
- **W1.** The QoS 1 resend (above). [the user]
- **W2. More twin builds on `iothub_task`.** Each `rules_config`, `set_hub_name` and desired patch now builds there, the GET's answer adds a second build per connect, and a refusal a retry. `build_twin_reported()` makes five provisioning reads, each with a 1 s timeout (`provisioning_manager.c:90, 792, 848, 873, 1412`), so a provisioning mutex held for long can stall `iothub_task` up to about 5 s in that pass, and the next pass's Phase 2 then runs past WP2d's budgets (t0 + 2 s for a sensor, t0 + 3 s for a valve flood). The same exposure already existed for the session and device-set twins, and a normal hold lasts milliseconds. Hardening: one locked read for the twin, or leave an owed twin owed while provisioning is busy. [WP3; bench: 15k item 14(g)(6)] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `505eddc`, `1762a65`, `fdb8c28`; one locked summary, nothing built while busy (5 s back-off), at most one 1 s wait per pass.*
- **W3.** On a pass with an owed twin and a leak, the twin is posted in the session block (`app_iothub.c:4348-4350`) before that pass's device events (Phase 3), so one extra publish goes out ahead of `leak_detected`. The close decision (Phase 2) is unaffected. Optional: post the owed twin after the device events. [WP3] *Closed by WP3 (15q): `505eddc`.*
- **W4.** Twin admission has no heap floor, unlike a health alert's (12 KB with a 4.5 KB block while TX is busy). A twin is about 0.5 KB, owed reports collapse into one, and twins share the 8. [none]
- **W5.** `s_twin_retry_ms` is not cleared when a new session's own report covers the owed one, so a `rules_config` or `set_hub_name` on a fresh session within 5 s of the old session's refusal reports at the end of that window (up to about 7 s). Late, never wrong. [none]
- **W6.** A provision's twin deferred by 8 or more queued items while a refusal's window is open: its snapshot can go out once TX is idle, before the retry-gated twin, contrary to `63c2252`'s "a provision's deferred twin still goes before its snapshot". Both conditions at once; the final twin is still current. [none]
- **W7.** A decommission-all with 8 or more items queued skips its pre-restart twin; the reported twin updates only at the connect after the restart and the DPS re-registration. [accepted]
- **W8.** The identical-payload dedupe takes two real messages that print identically within one second as one: an A-B-A flap (`leak_detected`, `leak_cleared`, `leak_detected` for one sensor, with the same battery and RSSI) loses the second `leak_detected`, and the cloud's last event reads `leak_cleared` until the event snapshot, within about 5 s, restores the state (`C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 says so). [the cloud; a per-boot counter in a later schema]
- **W9.** The deepest twin path on `iothub_task` is 48 B deeper; its peak is still the snapshot build (about 4.2 KB, 15n). [CP6 item 16]
- **W10.** While a twin stays owed (8 or more queued for long), `iothub_task` polls every 2 s instead of 30 s, as for a held snapshot: check for log spam and that `rules_engine_tick` behaves the same. [CP6 item 15]
- **W11. Wording:** the W line says "built again after 5 s" (5-7 s in practice), and the I line's "went first" means "was seen first". Both lines are new in WP2e and kept as written. [WP10 register]

**Closed by WP2e:** 15n's TC-2 (decided), SAFE-1 and R4's SAFE-1 part (8), TW-1 and R16 (fixed; W1 remains); 15i residual 3 (a twin report esp-mqtt refused was never sent again: now it is built again 5 s later); the twin lost to `TX queue full (24) - twin not sent`.

**Docs.** This docs commit: `C2D_COMMANDS.md` §3.5 (the dedupe rule, the pre-sync exception and how the cloud may also merge it, two real messages with the same bytes, the twin's order and delay, W1) and §4.11 step 2; `CHANGELOG.md` (the D3 note, the decided items, a WP2e entry); this handoff (the header, §1, §15's table, 15i residual 3, 15j-15l, 15n, 15o, this section); `RADIO_PORTAL_PLAN.md` §10's `.bss` note. Still to follow (WP10 register): `MANUAL_TEST_PLAN.md`'s header note, T6-11 and T6-21 still name `fcc0979` as CP6's commit (15j and 15k supersede them), and it has no WP2e step (15k item 14(g) has them); `docs/telemetry/catalogue_reference.py:324-325` says the offline buffer truncates an event over 512 B, while it refuses it whole (`offline_buffer.c:183-189`); 15n's list (`TELEMETRY_REFERENCE.md`, the generated `telemetry_messages.md`, `validate_capture.py`'s dedupe by identical body).

### 15q. WP3 core: the seven `sdkconfig` lines and their guards; membership in one hold; the busy-lock, twin, MQTT-stop and snapshot residuals (2026-10-06)

**Why.** Plan §11's WP3 (§4.9's seven `sdkconfig.defaults` lines with compile guards, D12), the user's two decisions of 2026-10-02 for WP3 (15o "For the user's decision" items 1-2: **(i)** keep a dry valve-flood report on a busy rules lock like a wet one; **(ii)** WP2D-C4, decide membership in the same provisioning hold as the rules), and the residuals earlier packages tagged for WP3: 15o residuals 2 and 3 (a flapping source, the swap's purge), 15p W2 and W3 (one locked provisioning read for the twin, the owed twin after the pass's events), 15n R18 with 15m residual 3 (a refused MQTT stop), and 15i residual 5 (a full hub's snapshot and the outbox). WP3's valve items (the stale-handle check and `REATTEMPT_COUNT`) are in the BLE stream's files and in 15s. Three streams ran in parallel tonight in one working tree; this one owned `main/main.c`, `sdkconfig.defaults`, `main/rules_engine`, `main/provisioning_manager`, `main/iothub` and `main/telemetry`.

**Commits** on `fix/2.1.4` after `1707588`: 11 implementer commits and 13 review fixes, each compiled alone in the scratchpad (`…\scratchpad\finish\wp3core\`, `…\wp3fix1\`) with the project's flags against a regenerated `sdkconfig.h`, no new warning, no IRAM, DRAM or RTC section. Nothing pushed, amended or rebased; line endings kept; every subject at most 72 characters, each message with the trailer.

The commit message gives the regeneration (15t) |
